On the rough solutions of 3D compressible Euler equations: an alternative proof
Huali Zhang, Lars Andersson

Abstract
. The well-posedness of Cauchy problem of 3D compressible Euler equations is studied. By using Smith-Tataru’s approach [ 32 ] , we prove the local existence, uniqueness and stability of solutions for Cauchy problem of 3D compressible Euler equations, where the initial data of velocity, density, specific vorticity v , ρ ∈ H s , ϖ ∈ H s 0 ​ ( 2 < s 0 < s ) v,\rho\in H^{s},\varpi\in H^{s_{0}}(2<s_{0}<s) . It’s an alternative and simplified proof of the result given by Q. Wang in [ 42 ] .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 76N10, 35R05, 35L60

1.
Introduction and Results

1.1.
Overview

In this paper we consider the Cauchy problem of compressible Euler equations in three dimensions, which is described as

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+\text{div}\left(\rho v\right)=0,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
v_{t}+\left(v\cdot\nabla\right)v+\frac{1}{\rho}\nabla p=0,\end{cases}

(1.1)

with the state function

p=p(\rho)=\rho^{\gamma}(\gamma>0),

and the initial data

\rho|_{t=0}=\rho_{0},\ v|_{t=0}=v_{0},

(1.2)

where
v=(v_{1},v_{2},u_{3})^{\text{T}},\rho
, and
p
, respectively, denote the fluid velocity, density, and pressure. The object is to answer the question that:
For which
s
, the problem (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) is well-posed if
(v_{0},\rho_{0})\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

This question has been well studied for the irrotational case and the incompressible case in two or three dimensions. For the 3D compressible Euler equations (
1.1
), the best known result of the corresponding question is proved by Q. Wang in
[
42
]
. We will discuss it in a different view.

1.2.
Background

The compressible Euler equations is a classical system in physics to describe the motion of an idea fluid. The phenomena displayed in the interior of a fluid fall into two broad classes, the phenomena of acoustics waves and the phenomena of vortex motion. The sound
phenomena depend on the compressibility of a fluid, while the vortex phenomena occur even in a
regime where the fluid may be considered to be incompressible.

For the Cauchy problem of
n
-D incompressible Euler equations:

\begin{cases}v_{t}+\left(v\cdot\nabla\right)v+\nabla p=0,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
\mathrm{div}v=0,\\
v|_{t=0}=v_{0},\end{cases}

(1.3)

Kato and Ponce in
[
20
]
proved the local well-posedness of (
1.3
) if
v_{0}\in W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),s>1+\frac{n}{p}
. Chae in
[
8
,
9
]
proved the local existence of solutions by setting
v_{0}
in Triebel-Lizorkin spaces. On the opposite direction, the ill-posedness of solutions was solved by Bourgain and Li
[
6
,
7
]
, in which they proved that the solution will blow up instantaneously for some
v_{0}\in H^{1+\frac{n}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),n=2,3
.

In the irrotational case, compressible Euler equations can be reduced to a special quasilinear wave equation (see Lemma
2.2
and taking
\varpi=0
). For general quasilinear wave equations:

\begin{cases}&\square_{h(\phi)}\phi=q(d\phi,d\phi),\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
&\phi|_{t=0}=\phi_{0},\partial_{t}\phi|_{t=0}=\phi_{1},\end{cases}

(1.4)

\phi
is a scalar function and
h(\phi)
is a Lorentzian metric depending on
\phi
, and
q
is a quadratic term of
d\phi
. Set the initial data
(\phi_{0},\phi_{1})\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. By using classical energy methods and Sobolev imbeddings, Hughes-Kato-Marsden in
[
17
]
proved the local well-posedness of the problem (
1.4
) for
s>\frac{n}{2}+1
. On the other side, Lindblad in
[
26
]
constructed some counterexamples for (
1.4
) when
s={\frac{7}{4}},n=2
or
s=2,n=3
. In mathematics, to lower the regularity, one may seek a type of space-time estimates of
d\phi
, namely,
Strichartz estimates. Rigorous mathematical study of Strichartz estimates was initiated by Bahouri-Chemin
[
3
,
4
]
and Tataru
[
34
,
35
]
respectively, who established the local well-posedness of (
1.4
) when
s>\frac{n}{2}+\frac{7}{8},n=2
or
s>\frac{n}{2}+\frac{3}{4},n\geq 3
. The same result was also obtained by Klainerman in
[
21
]
. Through introducing a vector-field approach and a decomposition of curvature, the 3D result of
[
3
,
4
,
34
,
35
]
was later improved by Klainerman and Rodnianski, where
s>2+\frac{2-\sqrt{3}}{2}
. Based on Klainerman and Rodnianski’s vector-field methods, Geba in
[
19
]
studied the local well-posedness of the 2D case for
s>\frac{7}{4}+\frac{5-\sqrt{22}}{4}
. In 2005, a sharp result was established by Smith and Tataru in
[
32
]
; they proved that the local solution of (
1.4
) is well-posed if the regularity of initial data satisfies
s>\frac{7}{4},n=2
or
s>2,n=3
or
s>\frac{n+1}{2},4\leq n\leq 6
. An alternative proof of the 3D result was also obtained through vector-field approach by Wang
[
41
]
. Besides, we should also mention substantial significant progress which has been made on low
regularity solutions of Einstein vacuum equations, membrane equations or quasi-linear wave equations due to Andersson and Moncreif
[
2
]
, Ettinger and Lindblad
[
6
]
, Klainerman and Rodnianski
[
23
,
24
]
, Q. Wang
[
38
,
39
,
40
]
, Allen-Andersson-Restuccia
[
1
]
, C.B. Wang
[
37
]
and so on.

In the general case, concerning to the compressible Euler equations in
n
-D, the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) is well-posed if
v_{0},\rho_{0}\in H^{s},s>1+\frac{n}{2}
and the density is far away from vacuum, please see Majda’s book
[
29
]
.
Recently, a wave-transport structure of the 2D and 3D compressible Euler equations is introduced by Luk and Speck
[
27
,
28
]
, in which their central theme is to describe a sharp asymptotic behavior of the singularity formation. Based on
[
28
]
, Disconzi-Luo-Mazzone-Speck in
[
14
]
proved
the existence of solutions with non-zero vorticity
\varpi
and entropy
S
, and the assumptions on the
initial data of velocity
v
, density
\rho
and
\varpi
is in
H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
S\in H^{3+},\mathrm{curl}\varpi\in C^{0,\delta}
with
0<\delta<1
. Independently, without the Hölder norm of
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
, Wang in
[
42
]
proved
the local well-posedness of the 3D compressible Euler equations with initial data of
(v,\rho,\varpi)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\ 2<s^{\prime}<s
. Recently, some related 2D result is obtained by Zhang in
[
43
,
44
]
. We should also mention substantial significant progress which has been made on shock formation and free boundary problem due to Sideris
[
31
]
, Christodoulou-Miao
[
10
]
, Luk-Speck
[
27
,
28
]
, Coutand-Lindblad-Shkoller
[
11
]
, Coutand-Shkoller
[
11
,
13
]
, Jang-Masmoudi
[
16
]
, Ifrim-Tataru
[
18
]
and so on.

1.3.
Motivation and contribution

In
[
14
,
42
]
, they proved the low-regularity well-posedness of (
1.1
) in 3D by vector field approach. There is also a different and significant view to consider the low regularity problem, which is established by Smith and Tataru in
[
32
]
. This motivates us to generalize Smith-Tataru’s result to 3D compressible Euler equations with non-trivial vorticity. It’s a non-trivial process for us to obtain the same result established by Q. Wang in
[
42
]
. Moreover, compared with
[
42
]
, our paper is much simplified. Our contribution is in the following,

(i) combining two formulations(a hyperbolic system and a wave-transport system) for compressible Euler equations, which shows a direct way to discuss some energy estimates.

(ii) deriving a modified transport equation for vorticity.

(iii) obtaining characteristic energy estimates for vorticity along null hypersurfaces, which is different from basic energy estimates and non-trivial.

(iv) introducing a modified Strichartz estimate for linear wave equation endowed with the acoustic metric, which is adapting the structure of compressible Euler equations.

Before stating our result, let us introduce some quantities and notations.

1.4.
Quantities and notations

Definition 1.1
.

Let
\bar{\rho}
be a constant background density and
\bar{\rho}>0
. We denote the logarithmic density
\bm{\rho}
and specific vorticity
\varpi

\bm{\rho}:=\ln\left(\frac{\rho}{{\bar{\rho}}}\right),\quad\varpi:={\rho}^{-1}{\mathrm{curl}v}={\rho}^{-1}\omega.

(1.5)

Definition 1.2
.

We denote the speed of sound

c_{s}:=\sqrt{\text{d}p/\text{d}\rho}.

(1.6)

In view of (
1.5
), we have

c_{s}=c_{s}(\bm{\rho})

(1.7)

and

c^{\prime}_{s}=c^{\prime}_{s}(\bm{\rho}):=\frac{\text{d}c_{s}}{\text{d}\bm{\rho}}.

(1.8)

Definition 1.3
.

We define the acoustical metric
g
and the inverse acoustical metric
g^{-1}
relative to the Cartesian coordinates as follows:

\displaystyle g:=-dt\otimes dt+c_{s}^{-2}\sum_{a=1}^{3}\left(dx^{a}-v^{a}dt\right)\otimes\left(dx^{a}-v^{a}dt\right),

(1.9)

\displaystyle g^{-1}:=-(\partial_{t}+v^{a}\partial_{a})\otimes(\partial_{t}+v^{b}\partial_{b})+c_{s}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{i}\otimes\partial_{i}.

(1.10)

We are ready to introduce a reduction of (
1.1
) by using the logarithmic density in Lemma
1.1
, which is first proposed by Luk-Speck
[
27
]
.

Lemma 1.1
.

[
27
]

Let
\varpi
and
\bm{\rho}
are defined in (
1.5
). For 3D compressible Euler equations, we can reduce (
1.1
) to the following equations:

\begin{cases}\mathbf{T}v^{i}=-c^{2}_{s}\partial^{i}\bm{\rho},\\
\mathbf{T}\bm{\rho}=-\mathrm{div}v,\end{cases}

(1.11)

where
\mathbf{T}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
.

For brevity, we set
\partial=(\partial_{1},\partial_{2},\partial_{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
,
d=(\mathbf{T},\partial_{1},\partial_{2},\partial_{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
in this paper. We denote the operator

\square_{g}:=g^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}=-\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}+c^{2}_{s}\Delta,

(1.12)

where
\Delta=\partial^{2}_{1}+\partial^{2}_{2}+\partial^{2}_{3}
. Introduce a decomposition for the velocity

v^{i}=v_{+}^{i}+\eta^{i},

(1.13)

where the vector
\eta=(\eta^{1},\eta^{2},\eta^{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
is defined by

-\Delta\eta^{i}:=\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}.

(1.14)

Let us denote

\left<\xi\right>=(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

Denote by
\left<\partial\right>
the corresponding Bessel potential multiplier. The symbol
\epsilon^{ijk}(i,j,k=1,2,3)
denote the standard volume form on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Following the book
[
5
]
, the symbol
{\Delta}_{j}
denotes the homogeneous frequency localized operator with frequency
2^{j},j\in\mathbb{Z}
. For
f\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we denote
\|f\|_{H^{s}}:=\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}
, where
\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}:={\sum_{j\geq-1}}2^{2js}\|{\Delta}_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}}
. We also denote
\|f\|^{r}_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,r}}:={\sum_{j\geq-1}}2^{jsr}\|{\Delta}_{j}f\|^{r}_{L^{p}}
. We set
\eta_{0}:=\eta(t,x)|_{t=0}
and

\delta_{0}=s_{0}-2,\quad\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

(1.15)

The notation
X\lesssim Y
means
X\leq CY
, where
C
is a universal constant. The notation
X\simeq Y
means
C_{1}Y\leq X\leq C_{2}Y
, where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are universal constants. We use the notation
X\ll Y
to mean that
X\leq CY
with a sufficiently large constant
C
.

We also assume
2<s_{0}<s<\frac{5}{2}
and use four small parameters

\epsilon_{3}\ll\epsilon_{2}\ll\epsilon_{1}\ll\epsilon_{0}\ll 1.

(1.16)

Now, we are ready to state the result in this paper.

1.5.
Statement of result.

Theorem 1.2
.

Consider the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)-(
1.2
). Assume that

c_{s}|_{t=0}>c_{0}>0,

(1.17)

where
c_{0}
is a positive constant. Let
s>s_{0}>2
. Let
\bm{\rho}
and
\varpi
be defined in (
1.5
). For any
M_{0}>0
and initial data
(v_{0},\rho_{0})
satisfying

\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0},

(1.18)

there exist positive constants
T_{*}
and
M_{1}
such that (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) has a unique solution
(v,\bm{\rho})\in C([0,T_{*}],H^{s})
,
\varpi\in C([0,T_{*}],H^{s_{0}})
.
To be precise,

\mathrm{(1)}
the solution
v,\bm{\rho}
and
\varpi
satisfy the energy estimate

\|v,\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{1},

\mathrm{(2)}
the solution
v,\bm{\rho}
and
v_{+}
satisfy the Strichartz estimate

\|dv,d\bm{\rho},dv_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq M_{1}.

\mathrm{(3)}
for any
1\leq r\leq s_{0}+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[0,T]
, the linear equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}f=\mathbf{T}G+B,\qquad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{0}\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad\mathbf{T}f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{1}\in H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\end{cases}

(1.19)

admits a solution
f\in C([0,T],H^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T],H^{r-1})
and the following estimates hold:

\|f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{r-1}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}.

(1.20)

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
k<r-1
,

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}.

(1.21)

Remark 1.1
.

The condition (
1.17
) is used to satisfy the hyperbolicity condition of the system (
1.1
).

Remark 1.2
.

The type of (
1.19
) is devised for adapting to the structure of the acoustic metric.

1.6.
A sketch of the proof

The first step is to obtain energy estimates. Different from Q. Wang’s paper
[
38
]
, we use the hyperbolic system (
2.1
) to derive the basic energy of velocity and density

\|v,\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{a}}\leq\|v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{a}}\exp({\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}}),\quad a\geq 0,

(1.22)

which shows that
\|v,\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{a}}
is independent of the vorticity. If
a>\frac{5}{2}
, then the classical commutator estimates and continuity method can be used to prove the well-posedness of the problem (
1.1
)-(
1.2
), one can refer Majda’s book
[
29
]
. However, there is no uniform space-time estimates of
{\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}}
for (
1.1
) if
a\leq\frac{5}{2}
. However, with trival vorticity(
\mathrm{curl}v=0
), the compressible Euler equations can be reduced to a special quasilinear wave equation, and there is a type of Strichartz estimates, i.e.
{\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}}
, which can have
\frac{1}{2}
-regularity decrease compared to the classical result by Majda. Then the regularity requires greater than
2
in this case, please refer Smith-Tataru’s paper
[
32
]
(also in Q. Wang’s paper
[
41
]
for an alternative proof). What’s the situation with non-trivial vorticity. Let us see Luk-Speck’s result
[
28
]
, where they introduced a wave-transport system (
1.23
) for 3D compressible Euler equations. In some extent, we can expect to lower the regularity of velocity and density if seeing it as a ”disturbed” wave equation, and the disturbance is described by a transport equation. In precise,
the wave-transport system is the following

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}v=-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\varpi+\mathrm{quadratic\ terms},\\
&\square_{g}\bm{\rho}=\mathrm{quadratic\ terms},\\
&\mathbf{T}\varpi=(\varpi\cdot\nabla)v.\end{cases}

(1.23)

Based on the wave-transport system (
1.23
), the wave equation of
v
and
\bm{\rho}
play a crucial role for it’s character. Then, it returns back to require some information
\varpi
or
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
. Because the transport equation for
\varpi
is not good as the wave equation, so we expect a Strichartz estimates
{\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}}
with a smallest regularity for
\varpi
. A key observation is that, seeing from the best known results
[
32
,
41
]
, we can hope that there is some Strichartz estimates if the regularity of
v,\rho
, and
W
is greater than
2
. We then set the initial data
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0})\in H^{s},s>2
and
\varpi_{0}\in H^{s_{0}},s_{0}>2
. Therefore, we need to establish the energy estimates for
\varpi
in the frame
H^{s_{0}}(s_{0}>2)
. Followed by
[
28
,
42
]
, we also set
\Omega=\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi
. Different from
[
42
]
, we derive a modified transport equation for
\mathrm{curl}\Omega
(see Lemma
2.4
):

\begin{split}\mathbf{T}\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\big)=&\partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\\
&+(\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho})\cdot\partial^{2}\varpi+(\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho})\cdot\partial^{2}v+\partial\varpi\cdot\partial\varpi.\end{split}

(1.24)

We consider the term
\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}
as a whole part, and then transfer the goal to obtain the estimates
\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}
. In this process, we need to handle the trouble term

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}\partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx.

By Plancherel formula, we can transfer derivatives in the way

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx.

After getting the desired energy estimates, we then use Young’s inequality to handle the lower order term
2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}
. Based on this and (
1.22
), we can obtain the following energy estimates

\begin{split}&\|(\bm{\rho},v)(t)\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi(t)\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim(\|(\bm{\rho}_{0},v_{0})\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(dv,d\bm{\rho})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})d\tau\big).\end{split}

One can see Theorem
2.4
, Theorem
3.1
, and Theorem
3.2
for details. In a word, the problem is concluded to bound the Strichartz estimate

{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(dv,d\bm{\rho})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}d\tau,

(1.25)

for some
t>0
. Here, we take
2<s_{0}<s
. Because, if we take
s=s_{0}
, then (
1.25
) cannot be proved if we refer
[
32
,
41
]
.

The second difficult step is the Stricharz estimate (
1.25
). To do that, we extend Smith-Tataru’s method
[
32
]
to compressible Euler equations, which is totally different from Q. Wang’s work
[
38
]
by using vector-field approach. Based on Smith-Tataru’s work, we first reduce the problem of establishing an existence result for small, supported initial data. Next, by the continuity method, we can give a bootstrap argument on the regularity of the solutions to the nonlinear equation. Then, by introducing null hypersurfaces, the key is transformed to prove characteristic energy estimates of solutions along null hypersurfaces, and the enough regularity of null hypersurfaces is crucial to prove the Strichartz estimate. To establish characteristic energy estimates, we go back to see the wave-transport system. Note the regularity of
\varpi
is only
s_{0}
. Then, we can only get the same level regularity
s_{0}
on characteristic hypersurfaces(see Section 6 for details). We use the wave equation of
(v,\bm{\rho})
to get these characteristic energy estimates on characteristic hypersurfaces. As for
\varpi
, the characteristic energy estimate is difficult. Let us explain it as follows. On the Cauchy slice
\{t=\tau\}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can use elliptic estimates to get the energy estimate of all derivatives of
\varpi
by using
\mathrm{div}\varpi
and
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
. However, on the characteristic hypersurface, these type of elliptic energy estimates don’t work. To go through this difficulty, we use Hodge decomposition to recover some transport equations for derivatives of
\varpi
, where it involves Riesz operator. Then, some commutator estimated for Riesz operator and
v\cdot\nabla
concerning to compressible fluid is required. Please refer Lemma
2.14
,
2.13
,
2.16
,
7.7
, and
7.8
for detials.

After obtaining enough regularity of null hypersurfaces and coefficients from null frame, we can reduce the problem to proving the Strichartz estimate of a linear wave equation endowed with the acoustical metric
g
. Precisely, for any
1\leq r\leq s_{0}+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[0,T]
, the linear equation (
1.19
)
admites a solution
f\in C([0,T],H^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T],H^{r-1})
and the following estimates holds:

\|f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big).

(1.26)

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
k<r-1
,

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big).

(1.27)

We still need to prove

{\int^{t}_{0}}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}d\tau.

It can not be obtained by using the wave equation of velocity, for the regularity of source term
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
is only
s_{0}-1
.
By using the wave equation for
v_{+}
(see Lemma
2.2
),

\begin{split}&\square_{g}\bm{\rho}=\mathcal{D},\\
&\square_{g}v^{i}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}+Q^{i},\end{split}

(1.28)

we can bound

{\int^{t}_{0}}\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}d\tau

by Littlewood-Paley decomposition. It crucially relies on the regularity of right hand terms can be reached to
s-1
. More precisely, operating
\Delta_{j}
on (
1.28
), we can derive that
\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}
and
\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}
satisfy

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}=\Delta_{j}\mathcal{D}+[\square_{g},\Delta_{j}]\bm{\rho},\\
&\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}_{0},\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}+\Delta_{j}Q^{i}+[\square_{g},\Delta_{j}]v^{i}_{+}+[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}]\mathbf{T}\eta^{i},\\
&\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}(v_{0}-\eta_{0}),\quad\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}v^{i}_{+}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}(v_{0}-\eta_{0}).\end{cases}

By using the Strichartz estimate in (
1.21
) (taking
r=s-s_{0}+1,k=0
) , we obtain

\begin{split}\|(\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho},\Delta_{j}v_{+})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\ &\mathrm{"Right\ hand\ side"}\end{split}

(1.29)

Multiplying
2^{(s_{0}-1)j}
on (
1.29
), and taking square of it and summing it over
j\geq 1
, we get

\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\bm{\rho},v_{+}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|v_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}.

Based on the above estimate, by using (
1.14
), we can conclude the proof. As for the precise proofs, please refer Section 7. The last step is to prove (
8.1
) and (
8.2
), which is presented in the Appendix part.

1.7.
Outline of the paper

The organization of the remainder of this paper is as follows. In Section 2, we give a treatment of hyperbolic-transport system and introduce energy estimates and stability theorem. In Section 3, we reduce our problem to the case of smooth initial data by using compactness methods. In the subsequent Section, using physical localized technique, we reduce the problem to the case of smooth, small, compacted supported initial data. In section 5, we give a bootstrap argument based on continuous functional.
In Section 6 we derive self-contained characteristic energy estimates along null hypersurfaces, which is used to prove the regularity of null hypersurfaces. In the next step, we give the proof of Strichartz estimates in Section 7. In the Appendix, referring
[
32
]
, we give a glue proof of Strichartz estimates of linear wave equations endowed with the acoustic metric.

2.
Preliminaries: commutator and energy estimates

2.1.
Some formulations to (
1.1
)

Let us first introduce a symmetric hyperbolic system to 3D compressible Euler equations.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
v,\bm{\rho}
be a solution of (
1.11
). Then it also satisfies the following hyperbolic system

U_{t}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}{A}_{i}(U)U_{x_{i}}=0,

(2.1)

where
U=(\bm{\rho},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
and

A_{1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}v_{1}&1&0&1\\
c_{s}^{2}&v_{1}&0&0\\
0&0&v_{1}&0\\
0&0&0&v_{1}\end{array}\right),\quad A_{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&1&0\\
0&v_{2}&0&0\\
c^{2}_{s}&0&v_{2}&0\\
0&0&0&v_{2}\end{array}\right),\quad A_{3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&0&1\\
0&v_{3}&0&0\\
0&0&v_{3}&0\\
c^{2}_{s}&0&0&v_{3}\end{array}\right).

We are ready to introduce a wave-transport reduction.

Lemma 2.2
.

[
27
]

Let
\varpi
and
\bm{\rho}
are defined in (
1.5
). We can reduce (
1.11
) to

\begin{cases}\square_{g}v^{i}=-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}+Q^{i},\\
\square_{g}\bm{\rho}=\mathcal{D},\\
\mathbf{T}\varpi^{i}=\varpi^{a}\partial_{a}v^{i}.\end{cases}

(2.2)

Above,
Q^{i}
and
\mathcal{D}
are null forms relative to
g
, which are defined by

\begin{split}Q^{i}:=&2e^{\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{i}_{ab}\mathbf{T}v^{a}\varpi^{b}-\left(1+c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}\right)g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}v^{i},\\
\mathcal{D}:=&-3c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\bm{\rho}+2\textstyle{\sum_{1\leq a<b\leq 3}}\big\{\partial_{a}v^{a}\partial_{b}v^{b}-\partial_{a}v^{b}\partial_{b}v^{a}\big\}.\end{split}

(2.3)

For brevity, we set
Q=(Q^{1},Q^{2},Q^{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
.

Remark 2.1
.

The equation (
1.11
) is derived from (
1.1
) via (
1.5
). The system (
2.1
), (
1.11
) and (
2.2
) is equivalent to (
1.1
) respectively. We will switch these equivalent systems from one to another without explanation.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
v
and
{\rho}
be a solution of (
1.1
). Let
\bm{\rho}
,
v_{+}
, and
\eta
be described in (
1.5
), (
1.14
), and (
1.13
) respectively. Then
v_{+}
satisfies

\begin{split}&\square_{g}v^{i}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}+Q^{i}.\end{split}

(2.4)

Proof.

First, by Lemma
2.2
, we have

\square_{g}v^{i}=-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}+Q^{i}.

Substituting (
1.13
) and (
1.14
) to the above equality, we then get

\begin{split}\square_{g}v_{+}^{i}=&-\square_{g}\eta^{i}-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}+Q^{i}\\
=&\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}-c^{2}_{s}\Delta\eta^{i}-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}+Q^{i}\\
=&\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}+c^{2}_{s}\left(-\Delta\eta^{i}-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}\right)+Q^{i}\\
=&\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}+Q^{i}.\end{split}

∎

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\varpi
be defined in (
1.5
). Then
\varpi
satisfy

\mathbf{T}\varpi=(\varpi\cdot\nabla)v.

(2.5)

If setting
\Omega=e^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi
, then

\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}=-2\epsilon^{imn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{m}v^{a}\partial_{n}\varpi_{a}+\epsilon^{amn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}v^{i}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n},

(2.6)

and

\mathrm{div}\varpi=-\varpi^{i}\partial_{i}\bm{\rho},

(2.7)

hold. Furthermore, the quantity
\mathrm{curl}\Omega
satisfies

\begin{split}&\mathbf{T}\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\big)=\ \partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)+\sum^{6}_{j=1}R^{i}_{j},\end{split}

(2.8)

where

\begin{split}R_{1}^{i}:=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial_{j}(\partial^{n}\varpi_{a})+\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial^{2}_{mj}\varpi_{n}\\
&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{j}v^{a}\partial^{ij}\varpi^{b}+\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\Omega_{k},\\
R_{2}^{i}:=&\ 2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}-\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\\
&\ -2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}v^{k}\partial_{k}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{i}+2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}\\
&\ -2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{m}\partial_{m}v_{a}+2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{i}\bm{\rho}\partial^{n}v^{a}\partial_{n}\varpi_{a},\\
R_{3}^{i}:=&\epsilon^{amn}\varpi^{i}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}+2\epsilon^{ajk}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\varpi_{k}\partial_{n}\varpi_{b},\\
R_{4}^{i}:=&\epsilon^{amn}\partial_{a}\varpi^{i}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}+2\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega^{a}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a},\\
R_{5}^{i}:=&2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}v^{k}\partial_{k}\varpi_{a},\\
R_{6}^{i}:=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\varpi^{m}\partial^{i}(\partial_{m}v_{a}).\end{split}

(2.9)

Proof.

The equations (
2.5
), (
2.6
), and (
2.7
) are derived in Luk-Speck’s paper
[
28
]
. Then, we only need to derive (
2.8
). The idea is mainly from Wang’s paper
[
38
]
. Here, the difference is that we give a modified transport equation, which is more convenient for proving energy estimates and character energy estimates in this paper. Let us first calculate
\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}
. Taking the operator
\mathrm{curl}
on (
2.6
), then we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}&=\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\mathbf{T}\Omega_{k}\\
&=\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\left(-2\epsilon_{kmn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}+\epsilon^{amn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}\right).\end{split}

(2.10)

Operating derivatives on (
2.10
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial_{j}(\partial^{n}\varpi_{a})+\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial^{2}_{mj}\varpi_{n}\\
&+2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{m}v^{a}\partial_{n}\varpi_{a}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}-\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\\
&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{ijk}\epsilon^{kmn}\partial_{j}(\partial^{m}v^{a})\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}+\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{2}_{aj}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}.\end{split}

For the last term, we use (
1.5
) to get

\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{2}_{aj}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}=\epsilon^{amn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\omega^{i}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}=\epsilon^{amn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\varpi^{i})\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}.

Hence, we can rewrite

\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}=A+R^{(1)}_{1}+R^{(1)}_{2}+R^{(1)}_{3}+R^{(1)}_{4},

(2.11)

where

\begin{split}R^{(1)}_{1}&=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial_{j}(\partial^{n}\varpi_{a})+\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial^{2}_{mj}\varpi_{n},\\
R^{(1)}_{2}&=2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}-\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho},\\
R^{(1)}_{3}&=\epsilon^{amn}\varpi^{i}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n},\quad A=-2\epsilon^{ijk}\epsilon_{kmn}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{j}(\partial^{m}v^{a})\partial^{n}\varpi_{a},\\
R^{(1)}_{4}&=\epsilon^{amn}\partial_{a}\varpi^{i}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}.\end{split}

Above, the term
A
is included the second-order derivatives of velocity, then we need to decomposition it. By using
\epsilon^{ijk}\epsilon_{kmn}=\delta^{j}_{m}\delta^{i}_{n}-\delta^{i}_{m}\delta^{j}_{n}
, we update
A
as

\begin{split}A&=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}(\delta^{j}_{m}\delta^{i}_{n}-\delta^{i}_{m}\delta^{j}_{n})\partial_{j}(\partial^{m}v^{a})\partial_{n}\varpi_{a}\\
&=A_{1}+A_{2},\end{split}

(2.12)

where

\begin{split}A_{1}:=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\Delta v^{a}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a},\quad A_{2}:=2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{i}(\partial_{j}v^{a})\partial^{j}\varpi_{a}.\end{split}

(2.13)

By Hodge decomposition, it tells us

\begin{split}\Delta v=\partial\mathrm{div}v-\mathrm{curl}^{2}v&=-\partial\mathbf{T}\bm{\rho}+\mathrm{curl}\big(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\varpi\big)\\
&=-\mathbf{T}(\partial\bm{\rho})+\partial v^{k}\partial_{k}\bm{\rho}++\mathrm{curl}\big(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\varpi\big).\end{split}

Substituting the above equality to
A_{1}
, we can update
A_{1}
as

\begin{split}A_{1}=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\big[-\mathbf{T}(\partial^{a}\bm{\rho})+\partial^{a}v^{k}\partial_{k}\bm{\rho}-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{ajk}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\varpi_{k}-\mathrm{e}^{2\bm{\rho}}\Omega^{a}\big]\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}\\
=&\mathbf{T}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}\big)-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\mathbf{T}(\partial^{i}\varpi_{a})\\
&+2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}+R_{2}^{(2)}+R_{3}^{(2)}+R_{4}^{(2)}\\
=&\mathbf{T}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}\big)-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\mathbf{T}(\partial^{i}\varpi_{a})+R_{2}^{(2)}+R_{3}^{(2)}+R_{4}^{(2)}+R_{2}^{(3)},\end{split}

(2.14)

where

\displaystyle R_{2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}v^{k}\partial_{k}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a},\quad R_{4}^{(2)}=2\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega^{a}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a},

\displaystyle R_{3}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=2\epsilon^{ajk}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\varpi_{k}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a},\qquad\ \ R_{2}^{(3)}=2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}.

In (
2.14
), it remains for us to write
-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\mathbf{T}(\partial^{i}\varpi_{a})
in a suitable way. Note
\mathbf{T}\varpi_{a}=\varpi^{m}\partial_{m}v_{a}
. Then we can calculate that

\begin{split}\mathbf{T}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}&=\partial^{i}(\mathbf{T}\varpi_{a})-[\partial^{i},\mathbf{T}]\varpi_{a}=\partial^{i}\varpi^{m}\partial_{m}v_{a}+\varpi^{m}\partial^{i}(\partial_{m}v_{a})-\partial^{i}v^{k}\partial_{k}\varpi_{a}.\end{split}

We therefore derive that

\begin{split}&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\mathbf{T}(\partial^{i}\varpi_{a})\\
=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\big(\partial^{i}\varpi^{m}\partial_{m}v_{a}+\varpi^{m}\partial^{i}(\partial_{m}v_{a})-\partial^{i}v^{k}\partial_{k}\varpi_{a}\big)\\
=&R_{2}^{(4)}+R_{5}+R_{6},\end{split}

(2.15)

where

\displaystyle R_{2}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{m}\partial_{m}v_{a},

\displaystyle R_{5}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}v^{k}\partial_{k}\varpi_{a},

\displaystyle R_{6}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\varpi^{m}\partial^{i}(\partial_{m}v_{a}),

Substituting (
2.15
) to (
2.14
), we have

\begin{split}A_{1}=\ &\mathbf{T}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}\big)+R_{2}^{(4)}+R_{6}+R_{5}+R_{2}^{(2)}+R_{3}^{(2)}+R_{4}^{(2)}+R_{2}^{(3)},\end{split}

(2.16)

At last, let us consider
A_{2}
. By using chain rule, we obtain

\begin{split}A_{2}=\ &2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{i}(\partial_{j}v^{a})\partial^{j}\varpi_{a}\\
=\ &\partial^{i}(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{j}v^{a}\partial^{j}\varpi_{a})+R_{2}^{(5)}+R_{1}^{(2)},\end{split}

(2.17)

where

\displaystyle R_{2}^{(5)}=2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{i}\bm{\rho}\partial_{j}v^{a}\partial^{j}\varpi_{a},\quad R_{1}^{(2)}=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{j}v^{a}\partial^{j}(\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}).

The commutator rule tells us

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}=\mathbf{T}\big(\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\Omega_{k}\big)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\mathbf{T}\Omega_{k}+\epsilon^{ijk}[\mathbf{T},\partial_{j}]\Omega_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\Omega_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}+R^{(3)}_{1},

where

R^{(3)}_{1}=-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\Omega_{k}.

Adding (
2.16
) and (
2.27
) to (
2.12
), and then combining with (
2.11
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\Omega_{k}.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a})+\partial_{j}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)

\displaystyle+R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3}+R_{4}+R_{5}+R_{6},

where

\begin{split}R^{i}_{1}=&R_{1}^{(1)}+R_{1}^{(2)}+R_{1}^{(3)}\\
=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial_{j}(\partial^{n}\varpi_{a})+\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial^{2}_{mj}\varpi_{n}\\
&-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{j}v^{a}\partial^{ij}\varpi^{b}+\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\Omega_{k},\\
R_{2}^{i}=&R_{2}^{(1)}+R_{2}^{(2)}+R_{2}^{(3)}+R_{2}^{(4)}+R_{2}^{(5)}\\
=&\ 2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon_{kmn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial^{m}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}-\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{amn}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{a}v_{k}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\\
&\ -2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}v^{k}\partial_{k}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{i}+2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a}\\
&\ -2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{m}\partial_{m}v_{a}+2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{i}\bm{\rho}\partial^{n}v^{a}\partial_{n}\varpi_{a},\\
R_{3}^{i}=&R_{3}^{(1)}+R_{3}^{(2)}=\epsilon^{amn}\varpi^{i}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}+2\epsilon^{ajk}\partial_{j}\bm{\rho}\varpi_{k}\partial_{n}\varpi_{b},\\
R_{4}^{i}=&R_{4}^{(1)}+R_{4}^{(2)}=\epsilon^{amn}\partial_{a}\varpi^{i}\partial_{m}\varpi_{n}+2\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega^{a}\partial^{i}\varpi_{a},\\
R_{5}^{i}=&2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}v^{k}\partial_{k}\varpi_{a},\quad R^{i}_{6}:=-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\varpi^{m}\partial^{i}(\partial_{m}v_{a}).\end{split}

At this stage, we complete the proof of (
2.8
).
∎

In the following, we will introduce some commutator estimates, product estimates.

2.2.
Classical commutator estimates and product estimates

Firstly, let us give some commutator estimates and product estimates, which has been introduced in references
[
20
,
32
,
38
]
.

Lemma 2.5
.

[
20
]

Let
\Lambda=(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}},s\geq 0
. Then for any scalar function
h,f
, we have

\|\Lambda^{s}(hf)-(\Lambda^{s}h)f\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{s-1}h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial f\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|h\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s}f\|_{L_{x}^{q}},

where
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
.

Lemma 2.6
.

[
20
]

Let
a\geq 0
. For any scalar function
h
and
f
, we have

\|hf\|_{H_{x}^{a}}\lesssim\|h\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|f\|_{H_{x}^{a}}+\|f\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|h\|_{H_{x}^{a}}.

Lemma 2.7
.

[
20
]

Let
F(u)
be a smooth function of
u
,
F(0)=0
and
u\in L^{\infty}_{x}
. For any
s\geq 0
, we have

\|F(u)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}).

Lemma 2.8
.

[
32
]

Suppose that
0\leq r,r^{\prime}<\frac{n}{2}
and
r+r^{\prime}>\frac{n}{2}
. Then

\|hf\|_{H^{r+r^{\prime}-\frac{n}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{r,r^{\prime}}\|h\|_{H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|h\|_{H^{r^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

If
-r\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
and
r>\frac{n}{2}
then

\|hf\|_{H^{r^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{r,r^{\prime}}\|h\|_{H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|h\|_{H^{r^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Lemma 2.9
.

[
38
]

Let
0\leq\alpha<1
. Then

\|\Lambda^{\alpha}(hf)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|h\|_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Lemma 2.10
.

[
38
]

Let
0<\alpha<1
. Then

\|\Lambda^{\alpha}(f_{1}f_{2}f_{3})\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|f_{i}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}\textstyle{\prod_{j\neq i}}\|f_{j}\|_{H^{1}}.

Lemma 2.11
.

[
30
]

Given
p\in[1,\infty]

such that
p\geq m^{\prime}
with
m^{\prime}
the conjugate exponent
of
m
. Let
f,g
and
\Phi
belong to the suitable functional spaces. Then

\|\Phi*(hf)-h*(\Phi f)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim\|x\Phi\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|h\|_{L^{p}}.

2.3.
Useful lemmas

Let us give some useful product estimates and commutator estimates, which play a crucial role in the paper.

Lemma 2.12
.

Let the metric
g
is defined in (
1.3
). If
f
is the solution of

\begin{split}\square_{g}f&=0,\quad t>\tau,\\
f|_{t=\tau}&=-G(\tau,x),\quad\mathbf{T}f|_{t=\tau}=-B(\tau,x),\end{split}

then

V(t,x)=\int^{t}_{0}f(t,x;\tau)d\tau

is the solution of the linear wave equation

\begin{split}\square_{g}V&=\mathbf{T}G+B,\\
V|_{t=0}&=0,\quad\mathbf{T}V|_{t=0}=-G(0,x).\end{split}

Proof.

We first note
\square_{g}=-\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}+c^{2}_{s}\Delta
. By calculating, we get

\begin{split}\mathbf{T}V&=\int^{t}_{0}\mathbf{T}f(t,x;\tau)d\tau+f(t,x;t)\\
&=\int^{t}_{0}\mathbf{T}f(t,x;\tau)d\tau-G(t,x).\end{split}

Taking the operator
\mathbf{T}
again, we have

\begin{split}-\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}V&=-\int^{t}_{0}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}f(t,x;\tau)d\tau-\mathbf{T}f(t,x;t)+\mathbf{T}G\\
&=-\int^{t}_{0}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}f(t,x;\tau)d\tau+B(t,x)+\mathbf{T}G.\end{split}

(2.18)

On the other hand,

c^{2}_{s}\Delta V=\int^{t}_{0}c^{2}_{s}\Delta f(t,x;\tau)d\tau.

(2.19)

Adding (
2.18
) and (
2.19
), we can derive that

\square_{g}V=\mathbf{T}G+B.

Furthermore, it satisfies

V|_{t=0}=0,\quad\mathbf{T}V|_{t=0}=-G(0,x).

We complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
0<\alpha<1
. Let
\beta>\alpha
and sufficiently close to
\alpha
. Then

\|hf\|_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|h\|_{C^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(2.20)

Proof.

Firstly, we have

\|hf\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}(hf)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}.

By using Bony decomposition, we get

\Delta_{j}(hf)=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}hS_{k-1}f)+\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}fS_{k-1}h)+\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}h\Delta_{k}f).

By Hölder inequality, we derive that

\begin{split}&\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}(hf)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\\
\lesssim&\ \textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}(\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\|S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\\
&\ +\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}(\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|S_{k-1}h\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\\
&\ +\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}(\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\Delta_{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\\
\lesssim&\ \textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}h\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}+\|h\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}\|\Delta_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}(\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}2^{2(-\beta+\alpha)}2^{k\beta}\|\Delta_{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}.\end{split}

(2.21)

It suffices for us to give the bounds of the last right terms on (
2.21
). Note

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}h\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim&\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j(-\beta+\alpha)}2^{2j\beta}\|\Delta_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\{2^{2j(-\beta+\alpha)}\}_{l^{1}_{j}}(\{2^{j\beta}\|\Delta_{j}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\}_{l^{\infty}_{j}})^{2}\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{\beta}_{\infty,\infty}}\lesssim\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|^{2}_{C^{\beta}}.\end{split}

(2.22)

We also note

\begin{split}&\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}\|\Delta_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}(\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}2^{2(-k\beta+j\alpha)}2^{k\beta}\|\Delta_{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\\
\lesssim&\ \textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}\|\Delta_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}(\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}2^{2(-k\beta+j\alpha)}2^{k\beta}\|\Delta_{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}})^{2}\\
\lesssim&\ \|h\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{\beta}_{\infty,\infty}}\textstyle{\sum}_{j\geq-1}2^{2j(\alpha-\beta)}\|\Delta_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|^{2}_{C^{\beta}}.\end{split}

(2.23)

Substituing (
2.22
) and (
2.23
) to (
2.21
), we can get (
2.20
).
∎

The next commutator estimate is concerning to Riesz operators.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
0\leq\alpha<1
. Denote the Riesz operator
\mathbf{R}:=\partial^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}
. Then

\|[\mathbf{R},v\cdot\nabla]f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Proof.

By using paraproduct decomposition, we have

\begin{split}\Delta_{j}[\mathbf{R},v\cdot\nabla]f&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left[\mathbf{R}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}S_{k-1}f\right]\\
&\quad+\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left[\mathbf{R}(S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}\Delta_{k}f\right]\\
&\quad+\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\Delta_{j}\left[\mathbf{R}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}\Delta_{k}f\right]\\
&=B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}B_{1}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left\{\mathbf{R}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}S_{k-1}f\right\},\\
B_{2}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left\{\mathbf{R}(S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}\Delta_{k}f\right\}\\
B_{3}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\Delta_{j}\left\{\mathbf{R}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}\Delta_{k}f\right\}.\end{split}

For
0\leq\alpha<1
, by Hölder’s inequality and Bernstein’s inequality, we can derive

\begin{split}\{2^{j\alpha}\|B_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}&\lesssim\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}(\|\nabla\mathbf{R}S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\nabla S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})2^{j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}v\|_{L^{2}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}2^{j\alpha}2^{k}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}v\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}(\|\mathbf{R}S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{2}}+\|S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{2}})\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}(\|\mathbf{R}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}})\lesssim\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{1+\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(2.24)

By Hölder’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\{2^{j\alpha}\|B_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}&\lesssim\{2^{j\alpha}\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}2^{k}\|\Delta_{k}v\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot 2^{-k}(\|\nabla\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{R}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{2}})\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}.\end{split}

(2.25)

Note

B_{2}=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}[\mathbf{R},S_{k-1}v\cdot]\Delta_{k}\nabla f.

By Lemma
2.11
and Hölder’s inequality, we get

\begin{split}\{2^{j\alpha}\|B_{2}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim&\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\|x\Phi\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla S_{k-1}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}2^{j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
\lesssim&\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\|\nabla S_{k-1}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}2^{j\alpha}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
\lesssim&\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}.\end{split}

(2.26)

Here, we use the fact that
{\Phi}=\frac{x_{i}x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}2^{3j}\Psi(2^{j}x)
and
\Psi
is in Schwartz space. Gathering (
2.24
), (
2.25
) and (
2.26
) together, we have finished the proof of Lemma
2.14
.
∎

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
2<s_{1}\leq s_{2}
. Then

\{2^{(s_{1}-1)j}\|[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}]f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v\|_{H^{s_{2}}}\|f\|_{{H}^{1}}.

Proof.

We first have

[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}]f=[\Delta_{j},v\cdot\nabla]f.

By using paraproduct decomposition, we have

\begin{split}[\Delta_{j},v\cdot\nabla]f&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\big(\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}\Delta_{j}f\big)\\
&\quad+\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\big(\Delta_{j}(S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}\Delta_{j}f\big)\\
&\quad+\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\big(\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}\Delta_{j}f\big)\\
&=A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}A_{1}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\left\{\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}\Delta_{j}f\right\},\\
A_{2}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\left\{\Delta_{j}(S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}\Delta_{j}f\right\}\\
A_{3}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\left\{\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}\Delta_{j}f\right\}.\end{split}

Notice
\mathrm{supp}\hat{A}_{1},\mathrm{supp}\hat{A}_{2}\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:2^{j-5}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{j+5}\}
. By Hölder’s inequality and commutator estimate, we can derive

\begin{split}\{2^{j(s_{1}-1)}\|A_{2}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}&\lesssim\left\{2^{j(s_{2}-1)}\|A_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&=\left\{2^{j(s_{2}-1)}\|[\Delta_{j},S_{j-1}v\cdot\nabla]\Delta_{j}f\|_{L^{2}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\left\{2^{j(s_{2}-1)}\|\nabla v\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\Delta_{j}f\|_{L^{2}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\|\nabla v\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}}.\end{split}

(2.27)

By Hölder’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\{2^{j(s_{1}-1)}\|A_{1}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}&\lesssim\left\{2^{j(s_{2}-1)}\|A_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&=\left\{2^{j(s_{2}-1)}\|\Delta_{j}v\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{6}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\|v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{2}-1}_{3,2}}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{6}}\\
&\lesssim\|v\|_{H^{s_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|v\|_{H^{s_{2}}}\|f\|_{H^{1}}.\end{split}

(2.28)

We rewrite
A_{3}
by the form

A_{3}=\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}[\Delta_{j},\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla]\Delta_{k}f.

Using Hölder’s inequality, we prove that

\begin{split}\{2^{j(s_{1}-1)}\|A_{3}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}&\lesssim\|\nabla v\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}}.\end{split}

(2.29)

Thus, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
0<\alpha<1
. Then

\|[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x},v\cdot\nabla]f\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Proof.

By using paraproduct decomposition, we have

\begin{split}\Delta_{j}[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x},v\cdot\nabla]f&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}S_{k-1}f\right]\\
&\quad+\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}(S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{k}f\right]\\
&\quad+\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\Delta_{j}\left[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{k}f\right]\\
&=V_{1}+V_{2}+V_{3},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}V_{1}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla S_{k-1}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}S_{k-1}f\right],\\
V_{2}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}\left[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}(S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{k}f\right]\\
&=\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\Delta_{j}[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x},S_{k-1}v\cdot\nabla]\Delta_{k}f,\\
V_{3}&=\textstyle{\sum}_{k\geq j-1}\Delta_{j}\left[\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}(\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Delta_{k}f)-\Delta_{k}v\cdot\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{k}f\right].\end{split}

For
0<\alpha<1
, by Hölder’s inequality and Bernstein’s inequality, we can derive

\begin{split}\{\|V_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}&\lesssim\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}(\|\nabla\Lambda^{\alpha}S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+2^{j\alpha}\|\nabla S_{k-1}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}})\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}v\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}2^{k}\|\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}v\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|S_{k-1}\Lambda^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}.\end{split}

(2.30)

By Hölder inequality, we have

\{\|V_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim\|\nabla v\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}.

(2.31)

By Lemma
2.11
(by setting
{\Phi}=2^{j(\alpha+3)}\Psi(2^{3}x)
,
\Psi
is in Schwartz space) and Hölder inequality, we get

\begin{split}\{\|V_{2}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim&\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}\|x\Phi\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla S_{k-1}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
\lesssim&\left\{\textstyle{\sum}_{|k-j|\leq 2}2^{j\alpha}\|\nabla S_{k-1}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{j}\Delta_{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
\lesssim&\|\nabla v\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|\nabla v\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}}.\end{split}

(2.32)

Gathering (
2.30
), (
2.31
), and (
2.32
), we can get Lemma
2.16
.
∎

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
2<s_{1}\leq s_{2}
. Let
\mathbf{g}
be a Lorentz metric and
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
. We have

\begin{split}\left\{2^{(s_{1}-1)j}\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim&\ \|df\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|df\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}}.\end{split}

(2.33)

Proof.

Let
{R}_{j}=[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]f
and
S_{j}=\textstyle\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq j}\Delta_{j^{\prime}}
. Note
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
. Then we can decompose
{R}_{j}
as

{R}_{j}={E}_{j}+{A}_{j}+{G}_{j},

where

\begin{split}E_{j}=&\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha i}f)-S_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f),\\
A_{j}=&\left(S_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})-\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\right)\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f),\\
G_{j}=&\Delta_{j}\big(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\big)\partial_{\beta}f.\end{split}

Consider

\Delta_{k}\Delta_{j}f=0,\quad\mathrm{if}\ |k-j|\geq 8.

Note the support of
\hat{E}_{j}
being in the set
\{\xi:2^{j-5}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{j+10}\}
, then

\begin{split}&\|E_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\lesssim 2^{j(s_{2}-s_{1})}\|E_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

For
A_{j}
, we first have

A_{j}=\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f).

By classical product estimates, we derive that

\begin{split}\|A_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}&\lesssim\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}(\|\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}+\|\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\|\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\|_{L^{\infty}}).\end{split}

(2.34)

By Bernstein’s ineuality, we can update (
2.34
) as

\begin{split}&\{2^{j(s_{1}-1)}\|A_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
\lesssim&\{\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}(\|\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\nabla\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{-j^{\prime}+j}\|\Delta_{j}(\partial f)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}}\\
&+2^{j(s_{1}-1)}\|\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\cdot 2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}(\partial f)\|_{L^{\infty}})\}_{l^{2}_{j}}.\end{split}

We set

\begin{split}N_{1}&=\{\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}\|\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\nabla\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{-j^{\prime}+j}\|\Delta_{j}(\partial f)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}},\\
N_{2}&=\{\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}2^{j(s_{1}-1)}\|\Delta_{j^{\prime}}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\cdot 2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}(\partial f)\|_{L^{\infty}})\}_{l^{2}_{j}}.\end{split}

By direct calculation, we have

N_{1}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}},\quad N_{2}\lesssim\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}.

In a result, we get

\left\{2^{(s_{1}-1)j}\|A_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}+\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}.

(2.35)

For
G_{j}
, by phase decomposition, we note

\begin{split}\Delta_{k}G_{j}=&\textstyle{\sum_{|k-m|\leq 2}}\Delta_{k}\left(\Delta_{m}\big(\Delta_{j}(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}))\big)S_{m-1}\partial_{\beta}f\right)\\
&+\textstyle{\sum_{|k-m|\leq 2}}\Delta_{k}\left(S_{m-1}\big(\Delta_{j}(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}))\big)\Delta_{m}\partial_{\beta}f\right)\\
&+\Delta_{k}\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{k}\big(\Delta_{j}(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}))\big)\tilde{\Delta}_{k}\partial_{\beta}f\right),\end{split}

where
\tilde{\Delta}_{k}=\Delta_{k-1}+\Delta_{k}+\Delta_{k+1}
.
By Hölder’s inequality, we can deduce

\begin{split}\|\Delta_{k}G_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \|\Delta_{k}\Delta_{j}\big(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\big)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(\|S_{k}\partial_{\beta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\Delta_{k}\partial_{\beta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&+\|S_{k}\big(\Delta_{j}(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}))\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{k}\partial_{\beta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

By Hölder’s inequality, we can deduce that

\begin{split}\|G_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{j}\big(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\big)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

In a result, we can get

\|G_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{j}\big(c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\big)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|c^{-1}_{s}\partial_{\alpha}(c_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{j}\partial_{\beta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

\left\{2^{(s_{1}-1)j}\|G_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}.

(2.36)

It remains for us to give a bound for
E_{j}
. By phase decomposition, we have

\begin{split}E_{j}=&\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha i}f)-S_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\\
=&\Delta_{j}\left(S_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\partial_{\alpha i}f\right)-S_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\\
&+\Delta_{j}\left(\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})S_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\right)+\Delta_{j}\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\tilde{\Delta}_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\right),\end{split}

where
\tilde{\Delta}_{j}={\Delta}_{j-1}+{\Delta}_{j}+{\Delta}_{j+1}
.
By commutator estimates, we have

\begin{split}\|E_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}&=\|[{\Delta}_{j},{S}_{j}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}]\Delta_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i})f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Delta_{j}\left(\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})S_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\right)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Delta_{j}\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{j}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i})\tilde{\Delta}_{j}(\partial_{\alpha i}f)\right)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{j}(df)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{j}(d\mathbf{g})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

In a result, we get

\left\{2^{(s_{1}-1)j}\|E_{j}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim\|\partial\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}+\|\partial f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{2}-1}_{x}}.

(2.37)

By combining (
2.35
), (
2.36
), and (
2.37
), we can get (
2.33
).
∎

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
\eta
be defined in (
1.14
). Let
\mathcal{D}
and
Q
be stated in (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) respectively. Let
s\in(2,\frac{5}{2})
and
2<s_{0}<s
. Then the following estimates

\|\mathcal{D},Q\|_{H^{s-1}}\lesssim\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{\infty}}(\|\bm{\rho},v\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}}),

(2.38)

and

\|\mathbf{T}\eta\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|\varpi\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}}\|v,\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s}},

(2.39)

hold. Moreover, the function
\eta
satisfies

\|\eta\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

(2.40)

Proof.

We recall the expression of
\mathcal{D}

\mathcal{D}=-3c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\bm{\rho}+2\textstyle{\sum_{1\leq a<b\leq 3}}\big\{\partial_{a}v^{a}\partial_{b}v^{b}-\partial_{a}v^{b}\partial_{b}v^{a}\big\},

which is described in (
2.3
). Using Lemma
2.6
, we can get

\|\mathcal{D}\|_{H^{s-1}}\lesssim\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial v\|_{H^{s-1}}.

(2.41)

Note

\begin{split}Q^{i}=&\ 2e^{\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{iab}\mathbf{T}v_{a}\varpi_{b}-\left(1+c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}\right)g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}v^{i}\\
=&\ -2e^{\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{iab}c^{2}_{s}\partial^{a}\bm{\rho}\varpi_{b}-\left(1+c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}\right)g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}v^{i}.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.6
, we can deduce that

\begin{split}\|{Q}\|_{H^{s-1}}&\lesssim(\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|dv\|_{L^{\infty}})(\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|dv\|_{H^{s-1}}).\end{split}

(2.42)

By using (
1.11
), then we have

\|dv\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s-1}}\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s-1}}\lesssim\|v\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s}}.

(2.43)

Combining (
2.43
), (
2.42
), and (
2.41
), we have proved (
2.38
). It remains for us to prove (
2.39
) and (
2.40
). By (
1.14
) and Sobolev imbedding, and elliptic estimates, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|\eta\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{s-2}}&\leq(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}})\|\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}},\end{split}

(2.44)

and

\begin{split}\|\eta\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{2}_{x}})\|\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(2.45)

Let us give a bound for
\mathbf{T}\eta
. By using (
1.14
), we get

-\Delta\eta=\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi=\mathrm{e}^{2\bm{\rho}}\Omega.

Then we have

-\Delta(\mathbf{T}\eta^{i})=\mathbf{T}\Omega^{i}-\Delta v^{m}\partial_{m}\eta^{i}-2\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial^{j}(\partial_{m}\eta).

(2.46)

By (
2.46
), (
2.6
), and Lemma
2.8
, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{T}\eta\|_{H_{x}^{s}}&\lesssim\|\partial v\cdot\partial\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{s-2}}+\|\partial^{2}v\cdot\partial\eta\|_{H_{x}^{s-2}}+\|\partial^{2}\eta\cdot\partial v\|_{H_{x}^{s-2}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+}}+\|\partial^{2}v\|_{H^{s-2}}\|\partial\eta\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}}+\|\partial v\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+}}\\
&\lesssim\|\varpi\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}}\|v,\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s}}.\end{split}

At this stage, we complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

3.
Basic energy estimates and stability theorem

In this part, we will prove the energy estimates and stability theorem.
Firstly, we use the hyperbolic system to give the basic energy estimate for density and velocity.

Theorem 3.1
.

(Basic Energy estimates for velocity and density)
Let
v
and
\bm{\rho}
be a solution of (
1.11
). Let
\varpi
be defines in (
1.5
). Then for any
s\geq 0
, we have

\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s}}+\|v\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\left(\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\right)\exp({\int^{t}_{0}}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau),\quad t\in[0,T].

(3.1)

Proof.

Using Lemma
2.1
, we have

\partial_{t}U+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(U)\partial_{i}U=0,

where
U=(\bm{\rho},v^{1},v^{2},v^{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
. Operating
\Lambda^{a}
on the above equality, we have

\begin{split}\partial_{t}(\Lambda^{a}U)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(U)\partial_{i}(\Lambda^{a}U)=-\sum_{i=1}^{3}[\Lambda^{a},A_{i}(U)]\partial_{i}U.\end{split}

(3.2)

Multiplying
\Lambda^{a}U
, integrating it by parts on (
3.2
), and using Lemma
2.5
, it yields to

\frac{d}{dt}\|\Lambda^{a}U\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|dU\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{a}U\|^{2}_{L^{2}}d\tau.

Integrating it on
[0,t]
and summing
a=0
with
a=s
, we can prove

\|U(t)\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|U(0)\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}\|dU\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big).

That is to say,

\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s}}+\|v\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\left(\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\right)\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big),\quad t\in[0,T].

∎

Secondly, by utilizing Lemma
2.4
, we can give basic energy estimates for vorticity.

Theorem 3.2
.

(Basic Energy estimates for vorticity)
Let
v
and
\bm{\rho}
be a solution of (
1.11
). Let
\varpi
be defined in (
1.5
) and
\varpi
satisfy (
2.5
). For
s_{0}>2
, the following energy estimates hold:

\|\varpi(t)\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}(\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})d\tau\big),

(3.3)

and

\|\varpi\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\|(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})\|_{H^{2}}\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau).

(3.4)

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1: lower-order energy estimate
. Recall that
\varpi
satisfies the transport equation (
2.5
). Multiplying
\varpi
and integrating it on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can get

\|\varpi(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau.

(3.5)

Using Gronwall’s inequality, we have

\|\varpi\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}\|dv\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big).

(3.6)

By elliptic estimates, we obtain that

\|\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{2}_{x}}\leq\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\mathrm{div}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}.

(3.7)

By Lemma
2.4
, and using Hölder inequality, we can prove

\|\mathrm{div}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}=\|\varpi\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq C\|\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{\frac{7}{4}}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}.

By interpolation formula and Young’s inequality, we can update it by

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{div}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq&C(\|\varpi\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\varpi\|^{\frac{3}{4}}_{{H}_{x}^{2}})\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\|\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{4}_{H^{1}_{x}}+\frac{c_{1}}{100}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.8)

Using
\mathrm{curl}\varpi=\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega
, we have

\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}=\|\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}=\|\mathrm{curl}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.

By Hölder’s inequality, we get

\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq C\|\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{\frac{7}{4}}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\leq C\|\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{4}_{H^{1}_{x}}+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.

(3.9)

Thus, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\leq&C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+C\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq&C\|\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{4}_{H^{1}_{x}}+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}+C\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}},\end{split}

(3.10)

and

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\geq&\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}-C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\geq&\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}-C\|\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{4}_{H^{1}_{x}}-\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.11)

Note (
2.8
). That is,

\begin{split}&\mathbf{T}\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\big)=\ \partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)+\sum^{6}_{j=1}R^{i}_{j}.\end{split}

Multiplying
\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}
on the above equality and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)\cdot\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx\\
&+\sum^{6}_{j=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}R^{i}_{j}\cdot\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx.\end{split}

(3.12)

Set

K_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)\cdot\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx,

(3.13)

and

K_{2}=\sum^{6}_{j=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}R^{i}_{j}\cdot\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx.

(3.14)

Integrating by parts, we can update
K_{1}
as

\begin{split}K_{1}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)\cdot\partial^{i}\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)\cdot\partial^{i}\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx,\end{split}

(3.15)

where we use the fact
\partial^{i}\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i}=0
. By Plancherel formula and Hölder’s inequality, we can prove

\begin{split}|K_{1}|=&\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}\varpi^{b}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{i}\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx\right|\\
\leq&C\|\partial v\partial\varpi\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\|\partial v\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{1}_{x}})\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\|v\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.16)

On the other hand, using (
2.9
), we can get

\begin{split}|K_{2}|\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}})\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}v\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.17)

Combining (
3.6
) to (
3.17
), we therefore have

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq&C(\|v\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}\\
&+C\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+C(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}})\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+C\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}v\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Integrating it from
[0,t]
, we can derive that

\begin{split}&\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}(t)-\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}(0)\\
\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}(\|v\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}})\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}v\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.18)

Consider

\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L^{2}}-C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.19)

Inserting (
3.9
) into (
3.19
), we can have

\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\geq\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L^{2}}-(C\|\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{4}_{H^{1}_{x}}+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}),

(3.20)

and

\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{L^{2}}+(C\|\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{4}_{H^{1}_{x}}+\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}).

(3.21)

Adding (
3.18
) and (
3.5
), and combining with (
3.10
), (
3.11
), (
3.20
), and (
3.21
), we can get

\|\varpi\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\|(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})\|_{H^{2}}\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau).

(3.22)

Step 2: higher-order energy estimate of
\mathrm{div}\varpi
. Note (
2.7
). Using product estimates in Lemma
2.8
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{div}\varpi\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-1}}&=\|\varpi\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-1}}\lesssim\|\varpi\|_{H^{2}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(3.23)

By interpolation formula, we can show that

\|\varpi\|_{H_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\varpi\|^{\frac{s_{0}-2}{s_{0}}}_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\varpi\|^{\frac{2}{s_{0}}}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.

(3.24)

Substituting (
3.24
) to (
3.23
), we then obtain

\begin{split}&\|\mathrm{div}\varpi\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-1}}\\
\lesssim\ &\|\varpi\|^{\frac{s_{0}-2}{s_{0}}}_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\varpi\|^{\frac{2}{s_{0}}}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}}}\\
\lesssim\ &\big(\|\varpi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\exp(\textstyle{\int^{t}_{0}}\|dv\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\big)^{\frac{s_{0}-2}{s_{0}}}_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\varpi\|^{\frac{2}{s_{0}}}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\big(\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\big).\end{split}

For
\frac{s_{0}-2}{s_{0}}\in(0,1)
, we then get

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{div}\varpi\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-1}}\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|^{\frac{s_{0}-2}{s_{0}}}_{L^{2}}\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|\varpi\|^{\frac{2}{s_{0}}}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\exp(2{\int^{t}_{0}}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau).\end{split}

By Young’s inequality, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{div}W\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-1}}\lesssim(\|\varpi_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}})\exp(4{\int^{t}_{0}}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)+\frac{1}{16}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.25)

Step 3: higher-order energy estimate of
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
. To get a estimate of
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
, we need to see the equation (
2.6
) and (
2.8
). Note

\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}=\|e^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}},

From (
2.6
), we known

\frac{d}{dt}\|\Omega\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\partial v\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\Omega\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}.

Thus,

\begin{split}\|\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}&\lesssim\|\Omega_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial W\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.26)

For
\Omega_{0}=\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi_{0}
, we then have

\|\Omega_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}.

Substituting the above inequality to (
3.26
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\Omega\|_{L_{x}^{2}}&\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{1}}.\end{split}

(3.27)

By elliptic estimates, we first note

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}=&\|\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}=\|\mathrm{curl}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\leq&\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\leq&\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.28)

Similarly, by using
\Omega=\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi
, we then get

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}&=\|\mathrm{curl}(\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(3.29)

Seeing from (
3.28
) and (
3.29
), we known the difference between
\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}
and
\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}
is only a lower order term
\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}
. Let us see what’s the bound of this lower order term. On one hand, we use Lemma
2.8
(taking
s_{1}=\frac{1}{2}+s_{0}-2,s_{2}=1
) to obtain

\begin{split}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}&\lesssim\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{0}-2}}\|\Omega\|_{{H}_{x}^{1}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}\|\Omega\|_{{H}_{x}^{1}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s}})\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{1}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s}})\|\varpi\|^{\frac{s_{0}-2}{s_{0}}}_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\varpi\|^{\frac{2}{s_{0}}}_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(3.30)

where we use the fact

\begin{split}\|\Omega\|_{{H}_{x}^{1}}&\lesssim\|\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s}})\|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{1}}.\end{split}

By using Young’s inequality, we can update (
3.30
) by

\begin{split}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}&\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s}})\|\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\frac{1}{16}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(3.31)

We are now in a position to consider
\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}
. Operating
\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}
on (
2.8
) give rise to

\begin{split}&\mathbf{T}\left(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\right)\right)\\
=&-[\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2},\mathbf{T}](\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a})\\
&+\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{1}\\
