&+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{2}+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{3}+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{4}+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{5}+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{6}.\end{split}

Multiplying
\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)
on the above equality and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\right)=\sum^{8}_{l=0}I_{l},\end{split}

(3.32)

where

\begin{split}&I_{0}={\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\partial_{k}v^{k}|\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\right)|^{2}dx,\\
&I_{1}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{1}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{2}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{2}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{3}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{3}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{4}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R^{i}_{4}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{5}=\textstyle{\sum_{i=1}^{3}}\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R_{5}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{6}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}R_{6}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{7}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx,\\
&I_{8}=\displaystyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}[\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2},v\cdot\nabla]\big(\text{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\big)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\left(\text{curl}\Omega_{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\right)dx.\end{split}

We will estimate these terms one by one. For
I_{0}
, by Hölder’s inequality, we can derive

\begin{split}|I_{0}|\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.33)

By using
R_{1}
in (
2.9
), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.9
, we have

\begin{split}|I_{1}|\lesssim&\|\partial v\cdot\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\big(\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\big)\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.34)

Using
R_{6}
in (
2.9
), Hölder’s inequality, Lemma
2.9
, and the product estimate in Besov spaces, we then get

\begin{split}|I_{6}|\lesssim\ &\|\partial v\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\partial^{2}v\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim\ &\big(\|\partial v\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial^{2}v\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial v\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}v\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\big)\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim\ &\big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\big)\|\partial^{2}v\|_{L^{2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\ +\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{2}v\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial^{2}v\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.35)

By using
R_{2}
in (
2.9
), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.8
, we get

\begin{split}|I_{2}|\lesssim&\|(\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho})\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\partial\Omega\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|(\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho})\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+\|(\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho})\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\Omega\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+(\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\Omega\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial\Omega\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.36)

Noting
R_{3}
in (
2.9
) and using Lemma
2.10
, we also have

\begin{split}|I_{3}|&\lesssim\|\partial v\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial v\|_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial v||_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\lesssim(\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial\Omega\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}..\end{split}

(3.37)

By using
R_{4}
in (
2.9
), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.8
, we can show

\begin{split}|I_{4}|\lesssim&\|\partial\varpi\cdot\partial\varpi\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{2s_{0}-1}{4}}_{x}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.38)

Recalling
R_{5}
in (
2.9
) and using Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.8
(taking
s_{1}=1,s_{2}=1
), we have

\begin{split}|I_{5}|&\lesssim\|\partial v\cdot\partial v\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&\lesssim\big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\big(\|\partial v\|_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}_{x}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}_{x}}\big)^{2}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.39)

It remains for us to handle the most difficult term
I_{7}
. Integrating
I_{7}
by parts, we get

\begin{split}I_{7}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\text{curl}\Omega^{i})dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a})dx\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\left\{\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\text{curl}\Omega^{i})\right\}dx\\
&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\text{curl}\Omega^{i}\big)dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a})dx\\
=&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\text{curl}\Omega^{i}\big)dx\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a})dx.\end{split}

(3.40)

We note

\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\text{curl}\Omega^{i})=\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\mathrm{div}(\text{curl}\Omega)=0.

(3.41)

Substituting (
3.41
) to (
3.40
), we can update it as

\begin{split}I_{7}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\big(\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\big)\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a})dx.\end{split}

By Plancherel formula, we can obtain

\begin{split}I_{7}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\xi^{i}|\xi|^{s_{0}-2}\widehat{\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot|\xi|^{s_{0}-2}\widehat{\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}d\xi\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\xi^{i}|\xi|^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{2}}\widehat{\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot|\xi|^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}\widehat{\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}d\xi\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)dx.\end{split}

By Hölder inequality and Lemma
2.8
, we can obtain

\begin{split}|I_{7}|\lesssim&\|\partial v\partial\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\\
\lesssim&(\|\partial v\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}})\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}}.\end{split}

(3.42)

It remains for us to give a bound for
I_{8}
. By using Lemma
2.16
, we can prove

\begin{split}|I_{8}|\lesssim&\|[\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2},v\cdot\nabla]\big(\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\big)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ \cdot\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.\end{split}

(3.43)

Combining (
3.33
), (
3.34
),… to (
3.43
), we can derive that

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\right)\\
\leq&C\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+C(\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial\Omega\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+C\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v\|_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{{H}^{1}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+C\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{2s_{0}-1}{4}}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+C\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+C\big(\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\big)\|\partial^{2}v\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+C\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}}+C\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\end{split}

(3.44)

Denote

K(t)=\|\text{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}.

Integrating (
3.45
) from
[0,t]
, we have

\begin{split}K^{2}(t)-K^{2}(0)\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial^{2}\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}K(\tau)d\tau+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}(\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\partial\Omega\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}K(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v\|_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{{H}^{1}}K(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{2s_{0}-1}{4}}}K(\tau)d\tau+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}K^{2}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\partial v\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\big)\|\partial^{2}v\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}K(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial v,\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{{s_{0}-1}}}K(\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

(3.45)

By Young’s inequality, we give the upper bound of
K(t)

\begin{split}K^{2}(t)\geq\ &\|\text{curl}\Omega\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}-C\|\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\geq\ &\|\text{curl}\Omega\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}-C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
\geq\ &\|\text{curl}\Omega\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}-C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}(\|\varpi\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial\varpi\|^{\frac{3}{4}}_{{H}^{1}})^{2}\\
\geq\ &\|\text{curl}\Omega\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}-C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{8}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{1}{100}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.46)

By Hölder inequality, we can give the supper bound of
K(t)

\begin{split}K^{2}(t)\leq\ &\|\text{curl}\Omega\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}+C\|\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial\bm{\rho}\cdot\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\leq\ &\|\text{curl}\Omega\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}}+C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|^{2}_{{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.\end{split}

(3.47)

Adding (
3.6
) and combining (
3.47
), (
3.46
), and (
3.45
), we therefore get

\begin{split}\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim&\ \|(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}\left(1+\|(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\right)\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big)\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|dv\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}})(1+\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\varpi\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}d\tau.\end{split}

Using Gronwall inequality, we can get (
3.4
). Hence, we complete the proof of Theorem
3.2
.
∎

Using Theorem
3.1
and
3.2
, we can derive the following theorem

Theorem 3.3
.

(Total energy estimates)
Let
v
and
{\rho}
be a solution of (
1.1
). Let
\bm{\rho}
and
\varpi
be defined as (
1.5
). Set the energy

E(t)=\|\bm{\rho}(t)\|_{H^{s}}+\|v(t)\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi(t)\|_{H^{s_{0}}},

and

E_{l}(t)=\|\bm{\rho}(t)\|_{H^{2}}+\|v(t)\|_{H^{2}}+\|\varpi(t)\|_{H^{2}}.

Then the following estimates

\begin{split}&E(t)\lesssim E(0)\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}(\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})d\tau\big),\end{split}

(3.48)

and

\begin{split}&E_{l}(t)\lesssim E_{l}(0)\exp\big({\int^{t}_{0}}(\|dv\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})d\tau\big),\end{split}

(3.49)

holds.

Based on these basic energy estimates, we are ready to give a stability theorem as follows.

3.1.
Stability theorem

Theorem 3.4
.

(Stability theorem)
Let
s>s_{0}>2
. Suppose that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
is a solution of (
1.11
) with initial data
v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0}
and there is a universal
C
such that

\|v,\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}}+\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq C.

Let
(\varphi,\psi,V)
be another solution to (
1.11
) with the initial data
(\varphi_{0},\psi_{0})\in H^{s},V_{0}\in H^{s_{0}}
such that

\varphi,\psi,V\in H^{s}
,
d\varphi,d\psi\in{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}
and
d\varphi_{+},d\psi\in L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}
. Then the following estimate

\begin{split}\|(v-\varphi,\bm{\rho}-\psi)(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|(\varpi-V)(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}&\lesssim\|(v_{0}-\varphi_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}-\psi_{0})\|_{H^{s-1}}\end{split}

(3.50)

holds.

Proof.

Let
U=(\rho,v)^{T}
and
B=(\psi,\varphi)^{T}
. Then

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}U+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U)\partial_{x_{i}}U=0,\\
&\partial_{t}B+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(B)\partial_{x_{i}}B=0.\end{split}

Then
U-B
satisfies

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}(U-B)+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U)\partial_{x_{i}}(U-B)=F,\end{split}

where

F=-\sum^{3}_{i=1}(A_{i}(U)-A_{i}(B))\partial_{x_{i}}B.

The standard energy estimates implies that

\frac{d}{dt}\|U-B\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C_{U,B}\left(\|dU,dB\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|U-B\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|U-B\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial B\|_{H^{s-1}}\right),

where
C_{U,B}
depends on the
L^{\infty}_{x}
norm of
U,B
. By using
v,\bm{\rho},\varpi,\varphi,\psi,V\in L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}
,
dv,d\bm{\rho},d\varphi,d\psi\in{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}
and
\partial V,\partial\varpi\in L^{\infty}
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|(U-B)(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-1}}&\leq C_{U,B}\|(U-B)(0,\cdot)\|_{H^{s}}\\
&\leq C_{U,B}\|(v_{0}-\varphi_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}-\psi_{0})\|_{H^{s}}.\end{split}

(3.51)

Since

\varpi=\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}v,\quad V=\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\psi}\mathrm{curl}\varphi,

then

\begin{split}\varpi-V=\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}(\mathrm{curl}v-\mathrm{curl}\varphi)+(\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}-\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\psi})\mathrm{curl}\varphi,\end{split}

we derive the following estimate by Lemma
2.8
:

\begin{split}&\|(\varpi-V)(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\leq\ &\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|\mathrm{curl}v-\mathrm{curl}\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}+\|\bm{\rho}-\psi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\|\mathrm{curl}\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(3.52)

For
s>s_{0}>2
, it follows that

\|\mathrm{curl}\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}=\|V\cdot\psi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\|V\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\cdot\|\psi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}.

By using elliptic estimate, we have

\|\mathrm{curl}v-\mathrm{curl}\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}\lesssim\|v-\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}.

The above estimates combining with (
3.52
) tell us that

\begin{split}\|(\varpi-V)(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}&\leq C_{\bm{\rho},\varphi}(\|v-\phi\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}+\|\bm{\rho}-\psi\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}})\\
&\leq C_{\bm{\rho},\varphi}\|(v_{0}-\varphi_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}-\psi_{0})\|_{H^{s-1}},\end{split}

(3.53)

where
C_{\bm{\rho},\varphi}
is a constant depending with
\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}
and
\|\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}
. From (
3.51
) and (
3.53
), we complete the proof of Theorem
3.4
.
∎

As a direct result of Theorem
3.4
, we can deduce:

Corollary 3.5
.

(Uniqueness of solution)
Let
s>s_{0}>2
. Supppose
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
and
(\varphi,\psi,V)
be solutions of (
1.11
) with initial data
v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}
satisfying
v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}\in H^{s},\varpi_{0}=\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}v_{0}\in H^{s_{0}}
. Then we have

v=\varphi,\quad\bm{\rho}=\psi,\quad\varpi=V.

4.
Reduction to the case of smooth initial data

In this part, we reduce Theorem
1.2
to the case of smooth initial data by compactness arguments.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
s>s_{0}>2
. For each
M_{0}>0
, there exists
T,M,C>0
such that, for each smooth initial data
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})
which satisfies

\begin{split}&\|(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0})\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0},\end{split}

(4.1)

there exists a smooth solution
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
to (
2.2
) on
[-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\|(v,\bm{\rho})\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M.

(4.2)

Furthermore, the solution satisfies the conditions

\mathrm{(1)}
dispersive estimate for
v
,
\bm{\rho}
and
v_{+}

\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|\partial v_{+},d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq M,

(4.3)

\mathrm{(2)}
Let
f
satisfy equation (
1.19
). For each
1\leq r\leq s_{0}+1
, the Cauchy problem (
1.19
) is well-posed in
H^{r}\times H^{r-1}
, and the following estimate holds:

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}},\ \ k<r-1.

(4.4)

In the following, we will use Proposition
4.1
to prove Theorem
1.2
.

proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Consider arbitrary initial data
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0})\in H^{s}
,
\varpi_{0}\in H^{s_{0}}
satisfying

\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq R.

Let
(v_{0}^{k},\bm{\rho}_{0}^{k},\varpi_{0}^{k})
be a sequence of smooth data converging to
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})
, which also satisfy the same bound. By Proposition
4.1
, there exists the corresponding solutions
(v^{k},\bm{\rho}^{k},\varpi^{k})
satisfying (
2.2
).

Note that the solutions of (
1.1
) also satisfy the symmetric hyperbolic system (
2.1
). Set
U^{k}=(\bm{\rho}^{k},v^{k},),k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
. For
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we then have

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}U^{j}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U^{k})\partial_{i}U^{j}=0,\\
&\partial_{t}U^{l}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U^{l})\partial_{i}U^{l}=0.\end{split}

The standard energy estimates implies that

\frac{d}{dt}\|U^{j}-U^{l}\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C_{U^{j},U^{l}}\left(\|dU^{j},dU^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|U^{j}-U^{l}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|U^{j}-U^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial U^{l}\|_{H^{s-1}}\right),

where
C_{U^{j},U^{l}}
depends on the
L^{\infty}_{x}
norm of
U^{j},U^{l}
. By Strichartz estimates of
dv^{k},d\bm{\rho}^{k},k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
in Proposition
4.1
, we could derive that

\begin{split}\|(U^{j}-U^{l})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-1}}&\lesssim\|(U^{j}-U^{l})(0,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-1}}\\
&\lesssim\|(v_{0}^{j}-v_{0}^{l},\bm{\rho}^{j}_{0}-\bm{\rho}^{l}_{0})\|_{H^{s-1}}.\end{split}

As a result,
\{(v^{k},\bm{\rho}^{k})\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([-T,T];H^{s-1})
. Denote

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(v^{k},\bm{\rho}^{k})=(v,\bm{\rho}).

Thus,
v,\bm{\rho}
is in
C([-T,T];H^{s-1})
. Since

\varpi^{k}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}^{k}}\mathrm{curl}v^{k},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}.

Due to product estimates and elliptic estimates, we have

\begin{split}\|\varpi^{k}-\varpi^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}}&\lesssim(\|v^{k}-v^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}+\|\bm{\rho}^{k}-\bm{\rho}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}})\\
&\lesssim(\|v_{0}^{k}-v_{0}^{l}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}^{k}_{0}-\bm{\rho}^{l}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}).\end{split}

Above, in the last line we use Lemma
2.7
. This implies that
\varpi^{k}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([-T,T];H^{s_{0}-2})
. We denote the limit

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\varpi^{k}=\varpi,

and
W
is in
C([-T,T];H^{s_{0}-2})
.

Since
v^{k},\bm{\rho}^{k}
is uniformly bounded in
C([-T,T];H^{s})
and
\varpi^{k}
is bounded in
C([-T,T];H^{s_{0}})
respectively. Thus,
(v,\bm{\rho})\in C([-T,T];H^{s}),\varpi\in C([-T,T];H^{s_{0}})
. On the other hand, using Proposition
4.1
,
dv^{k}
and
d\bm{\rho}^{k}
are uniformly bounded in
L^{2}([-T,T];C^{\delta})
. Consequently,
\{(dv^{k},d\bm{\rho}^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
converges to
(dv,d\bm{\rho})
in
L^{2}([-T,T];L^{\infty})
.

We also have
dv^{k}
and
d\bm{\rho}^{k}
bounded in
L^{2}([-T,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
. This combines with
\{(dv^{k},d\bm{\rho}^{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
converging to
(dv,d\bm{\rho})
in
L^{2}([-T,T];L^{\infty})
. We have
dv,d\bm{\rho}\in L^{2}([-T,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
. Set

v=v_{+}+\eta,\quad\eta=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi).

We can derive

\begin{split}\|\partial v_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\eta\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}.\end{split}

(4.5)

By Sobolev inequality, elliptic estimate, and (
3.49
), we have

\begin{split}\|\partial\eta\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}&\lesssim\|\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi)\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{2}})\|\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{2}})\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\exp{(2\int^{t}_{0}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)}.\end{split}

As a result, we have
\partial\eta\in L^{2}([-T,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
. So we can obtain
\partial v_{+}\in L^{2}([-T,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
. At this stage, we complete of proof of Theorem
1.2
.
∎

5.
Reduction to existence for small, smooth, compactly supported data

In this section, our goal is to give a reduction of Proposition
4.1
to the existence for small, smooth, compactly supported data using physical localization arguments.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
s>s_{0}>2
. Assume (
1.16
) and (
1.15
) hold. Suppose the initial data
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})
be smooth, supported in
B(0,c+2)
and satisfying

\begin{split}&\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(5.1)

Then the Cauchy problem (
2.2
) admits a smooth solution
v,\bm{\rho},\varpi
on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, which have the following properties:

\mathrm{(1)}
energy estimate

\begin{split}&\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(5.2)

\mathrm{(2)}
dispersive estimate for
v
and
\bm{\rho}

\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(5.3)

\mathrm{(3)}
dispersive estimate for the linear equation

Let
f
satisfy
the equation (
1.19
).
For each
1\leq r\leq s_{0}+1
, the Cauchy problem (
1.19
) is well-posed in
H^{r}\times H^{r-1}
, and the following estimate holds:

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}},\ \ k<r-1.

(5.4)

proof of Proposition
5.1
by Proposition
4.1
.

In Proposition
4.1
, the corresponding statement is considering the small, supported data. While, the initial data is large in Proposition
5.1
. Firstly, by using a scaling method, we can reduce the initial data in Proposition
5.1
to be small.

Step 1: Scaling
. Assume

\begin{split}&\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}.\end{split}

(5.5)

If taking the scaling

\begin{split}&\underline{{v}}(t,x)=v(Tt,Tx),\quad\underline{\bm{\rho}}(t,x)=\bm{\rho}(Tt,Tx),\quad\underline{\varpi}(t,x)={\varpi}(Tt,Tx)\end{split}

we then obtain

\begin{split}&\|\underline{v}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}+\|\underline{\bm{\rho}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}},\qquad\|\underline{\varpi}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}.\end{split}

Let
\epsilon_{3}
be stated in (
1.16
). Choose sufficiently small
T
such that

M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}\ll\epsilon_{3}.

Therefore, we get

\begin{split}&\|\underline{v}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\quad\|\underline{\bm{\rho}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\quad\|\underline{\varpi}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

Secondly, to reduce the initial data with support set, we need a physical localization technique.

Step 2: Localization
.

The propagation speed of (
2.2
) is also finite. We then let
c
be the largest speed of (
2.2
). Set
\chi
be a smooth function supported in
B(0,c+2)
, and which equals
1
in
B(0,c+1)
. For given
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we define the localized initial data for the velocity and density near
y
:

\begin{split}\widetilde{v}_{0}(x)=&\chi(x-y)\left(v_{0}(x)-v_{0}(y)\right),\\
\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}_{0}(x)=&\chi(x-y)\left(\bm{\rho}_{0}(x)-\bm{\rho}_{0}(y)\right).\end{split}

Based on these quantities
v^{y}_{0}
and
\bm{\rho}_{0}^{y}
. Followed by (
1.5
), we set

\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}=\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{v}_{0}=\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}_{0}}\widetilde{\omega}_{0},

(5.6)

then
\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}
is the specific vorticity.
Since
s,s_{0}\in(2,\frac{5}{2})
, we can verify

\|\widetilde{v}_{0},\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.

(5.7)

By calculation on (
5.6
), we have

\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}_{0}}\nabla\chi(x-y)\cdot(v_{0}(x)-v_{0}(y))+\chi(x-y)\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}{v}_{0}.

(5.8)

Hence, we derive that

\|\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\partial v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.

(5.9)

Substituting
\varpi_{0}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}v_{0}
into (
5.8
), we can update (
5.8
) as

\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}_{0}}}\nabla\chi(x-y)\cdot(v_{0}(x)-v_{0}(y))+\chi(x-y)\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}_{0}}+\bm{\rho}_{0}}\varpi_{0}.

Therefore, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}&=\|\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}_{0}}}\nabla\chi(x-y)\cdot(v_{0}(x)-v_{0}(y))+\chi(x-y)\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}_{0}}+\bm{\rho}_{0}}\varpi_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\varpi_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|v_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(5.10)

Adding (
5.9
) and (
5.10
), we can get

\|\widetilde{\varpi}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.

(5.11)

By Proposition
5.1
, there is a smooth solution
(\widetilde{v},\widetilde{\bm{\rho}},\widetilde{\varpi})
on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying the following equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\widetilde{v}=-\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}}\tilde{c}^{2}_{s}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\varpi}+\widetilde{Q},\\
&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{D}},\\
&\mathbf{T}\widetilde{\varpi}=(\widetilde{\varpi}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{v}.\end{cases}

(5.12)

Above,
\tilde{c}^{2}_{s},\widetilde{\mathcal{D}},\widetilde{Q}
, and
\tilde{{g}}
are defined by

\begin{split}\tilde{c}^{2}_{s}:&=\frac{dp(\widetilde{\bm{\rho}})}{d\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}},\quad\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{s}:=\frac{d\tilde{c}_{s}}{d\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}}\\
\widetilde{Q}:&=2e^{\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}}\epsilon^{i}_{ab}\mathbf{T}\widetilde{v}^{a}(\widetilde{\varpi})^{b}-\left(1+\tilde{c}_{s}^{-1}\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{s}\right)\tilde{{g}}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v},\\
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}:&=-3\tilde{c}_{s}^{-1}\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{s}\tilde{{g}}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}+2\textstyle{\sum_{1\leq a<b\leq 3}}\big\{\partial_{a}\widetilde{v}^{a}\partial_{b}\widetilde{v}^{b}-\partial_{a}(\widetilde{v})^{b}\partial_{b}(\widetilde{v})^{a}\big\}\\
\widetilde{g}:&=-dt\otimes dt+\tilde{c}_{s}^{-2}(\bm{\rho}^{y})\textstyle{\sum_{a=1}^{3}}\left(dx^{a}-\widetilde{v}^{a}dt\right)\otimes\left(dx^{a}-\widetilde{v}^{a}dt\right),\end{split}

(5.13)

and the specific vorticity
\widetilde{\varpi}
satisfies

\widetilde{\varpi}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{v}.

(5.14)

Set

\widetilde{v}_{+}:=\widetilde{v}-\widetilde{\eta},\quad\Delta\widetilde{\eta}:=-\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\varpi}.

(5.15)

By Proposition
5.1
again, we can find the solution of (
5.13
) satisfying

\|\widetilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\varpi}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(5.16)

and

\|d\widetilde{v},d\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\widetilde{v},\partial\widetilde{v}_{+},d\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{2,\infty}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(5.17)

Furthermore, the linear equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\tilde{f}=\mathbf{T}\widetilde{G}+\widetilde{B},\\
&\tilde{f}(t_{0},\cdot)=\widetilde{f}_{0},\ \mathbf{T}\tilde{f}(t_{0},)=\widetilde{f}_{1},\end{cases}

(5.18)

admits a solution
\widetilde{f}\in C([0,T],H^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T],H^{r-1})
, and the following estimate holds:

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|\widetilde{G}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|\widetilde{B}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}},\ \ k<r-1.

(5.19)

Consequently,
(\widetilde{v}+v_{0}(y),\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}+\bm{\rho}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\varpi})
is also a solution of (
5.12
), and its initial data coincides with
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})
in
B(y,c+1)
. Giving the restrictions, for
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\left(\widetilde{v}+v_{0}(y)\right)|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\quad\left(\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}+\bm{\rho}_{0}(y)\right)|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\quad\widetilde{\varpi}|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},

(5.20)

where
\mathrm{K}^{y}=\left\{(t,x):ct+|x-y|\leq c+1,|t|<1\right\}
, then the restrictions (
5.20
) solve (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. By finite speed of propagation, a smooth solution
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
solves (
2.2
) in
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
v,\bm{\rho}
and
\varpi
is denoted by

\begin{split}v(t,x)&=\widetilde{v}+v_{0}(y),\ \ \ (t,x)\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\bm{\rho}(t,x)&=\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}+\bm{\rho}_{0}(y),\ \ \ (t,x)\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\varpi(t,x)&=\widetilde{\varpi},\ \ \qquad\ \quad(t,x)\in\mathrm{K}^{y}.\end{split}

(5.21)

We also can obtain the initial information

(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)|_{t=0}=(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0}).

Therefore, the function
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
defined in (
5.21
) is the solution of (
2.2
) according to the uniqueness of solutions, i.e. Corollary
3.5
. By (
5.16
) and (
5.17
), we can obtain that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
satisfies (
4.2
) and (
4.3
), which is stated in Proposition
4.1
. It remains for us to prove (
5.4
) in Proposition
4.1
. Consider the solution
{f}
for

\begin{cases}&\square_{{{g}}}{f}=\mathbf{T}G+B,\\
&{f}(0)=f_{0},\ \mathbf{T}{f}(0)=f_{1}.\end{cases}

(5.22)

Take

\begin{split}&\tilde{f}_{0}=\chi(x-y)f_{0},\quad\tilde{f}_{1}=\chi(x-y)f_{1},\\
&\tilde{G}=\chi(x-y)G,\quad\tilde{B}=\chi(x-y)B.\end{split}

By finite speed of propagation, we can conclude that
\tilde{f}=f
in
\mathrm{K}_{y}
.
We write
{f}
as

{f}(t,x)=\textstyle{\sum_{y\in n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{n}}}\psi(x-y)\widetilde{f}(t,x).

For
1\leq r\leq 1+s_{0}
and
k<r-1
, using (
5.4
), it follows that

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}{f}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&\textstyle{\sum_{y\in n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{n}}}\|\psi(x-y)\left<\partial\right>^{k}\tilde{f}(x,t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\textstyle{\sum_{y\in n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{n}}}\big(\|\chi(x-y)f_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{r}\times H^{r}}+\|\chi(x-y)f_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{r}\times H^{r-1}}\\
&+\|\chi(x-y)G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|\chi(x-y)B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big),\\
\lesssim&\|f_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{r}\times H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{H^{r}\times H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}.\end{split}

At this stage, we have finished the proof.
∎

6.
A bootstrap argument

Let
\mathbf{m}
be a standard Minkowski metric satisfying

\mathbf{m}^{00}=-1,\quad\mathbf{m}^{ij}=\delta^{ij},\quad i,j=1,2,3.

Taking
v=0,\bm{\rho}=0
in
g
, the inverse matrix of the metric
g
is

g^{-1}(0)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}-1&0&0&0\\
0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0&0\\
0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0\\
0&0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)\end{array}\right).

By a linear change of coordinates which preserves
dt
, we may assume that
g^{\alpha\beta}(0)=\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}
. Let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function supported in the region
B(0,3+2c)\times[-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
, which equals to
1
in the region
B(0,2+2c)\times[-1,1]
. Set

\mathbf{g}=\chi(t,x)(g-g(0))+g(0).

Here

{g}=-dt\otimes dt+c_{s}^{-2}(\bm{\rho})\sum_{a=1}^{3}\left(dx^{a}-v^{a}dt\right)\otimes\left(dx^{a}-v^{a}dt\right).

Consider the following system

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}v^{i}=-\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\varpi^{i}+Q^{i},\\
\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bm{\rho}=\mathcal{D},\\
\mathbf{T}\varpi=(\varpi\cdot\nabla)v,\end{cases}

(6.1)

where
Q^{i},E^{i}
, and
\mathcal{D}
are null forms relative to
\mathbf{g}
, which are defined by

\begin{split}Q^{i}&:=2e^{\bm{\rho}}\epsilon^{i}_{ab}\mathbf{T}v^{a}W^{b}-\left(1+c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}\right)\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}v^{i},\\
\mathcal{D}&:=-3c_{s}^{-1}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\bm{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\bm{\rho}+2\textstyle{\sum_{1\leq a<b\leq 3}}\big\{\partial_{a}v^{a}\partial_{b}v^{b}-\partial_{a}v^{b}\partial_{b}v^{a}\big\}.\end{split}

(6.2)

We denote by
\mathcal{H}
the family of smooth solutions
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)
to Equation (
6.1
) for
t\in[-2,2]
, with the initial data
(v_{0},\bm{\rho}_{0},\varpi_{0})
supported in
B(0,2+c)
, and for which

\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3},

(6.3)

\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2},

(6.4)

\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2}.

(6.5)

Therefore, the bootstrap argument can be stated as follows:

Proposition 6.1
.

Assume that (
1.16
) holds. Then there is a continuous functional
G:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
, satisfying
G(0)=0
, so that for each
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
the following hold:

\mathrm{(1)}
The function
v,\bm{\rho}
, and
\varpi
satisfies
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

\mathrm{(2)}
The following estimate holds,

\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(6.6)

\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|\partial v_{+},d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(6.7)

\mathrm{(3)}
For
1\leq k\leq s_{0}+1
, the equation (
1.19
) endowed with the metric
\mathbf{g}
is well-posed in
H^{r}\times H^{r-1}
, and the Strichartz estimates (
5.4
) hold.

proof of Proposition
5.1
by Proposition
6.1
.

By using the standard continuity method in
[
32
]
, we can prove Proposition
5.1
by Proposition
6.1
.
∎

7.
Regularity of the characteristic hypersurface

In this part, we will prove Proposition
6.1
by analysing the regularity of characteristic hypersurface and Strichartz estimates of linear wave equations, which is stated in Proposition
7.2
, Proposition
8.1
, and Proposition
8.3
. This well-known strategy is proposed by Smith-Tataru
[
32
]
. The functional
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq\epsilon_{1}
will be defined later, and one can see (
7.2
) for details. Let
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
, and the corresponding metric
\mathbf{g}
which equals the Minkowski metric for
t\in[-2,-\frac{3}{2}]
. Let
\Gamma_{\theta}
be the flowout of this section under the Hamiltonian flow of
\mathbf{g}
. For each
\theta
, the null Lagrangian manifold
\Gamma_{\theta}
is the graph of a null covector field given by
dr_{\theta}
, where
r_{\theta}
is a smooth extension of
\theta\cdot x-t
, and that the level sets of
r_{\theta}
are small perturbations of the level sets of the function
\theta\cdot x-t
in a certain norm captured by
G
. We also let
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
for
r\in\mathbb{R}
denote the level sets of
r_{\theta}
. The characteristic hypersurface
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is thus the flowout of the set
\theta\cdot x=r-2
along the null geodesic flow in the direction
\theta
at
t=-2
.

Let us introduce an orthonormal sets of coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by setting
x_{\theta}=\theta\cdot x
. Let
x^{\prime}_{\theta}
be given orthonormal coordinates on the hyperplane prependicular to
\theta
, which then define coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by projection along
\theta
. Then
(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
induce the coordinates on
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
, and
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is given by

\Sigma_{\theta,r}=\left\{(t,x):x_{\theta}-\phi_{\theta,r}=0\right\}

for a smooth function
\phi_{\theta,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
. We now introduce two norms for functions defined on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\begin{split}&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|u|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}=\sup_{-2\leq t\leq 2}\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\|\partial_{t}^{j}u(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-j}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\\
&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|u|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2}=\big(\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\int^{2}_{-2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}u(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-j}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}dt\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(7.1)

The same notation applies for functions in
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We denote

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|_{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2},

where the right hand side is the norm of the restriction of
f
to
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
, taken over the
(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
variablles used to parametrise
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
. Similarly, the notation

\|f\|_{H^{s_{0}}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}

denotes the
H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
norm of
f
restricted to the time
t
slice of
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
using the
x^{\prime}_{\theta}
coordinates on
{\Sigma^{t}_{\theta,r}}
.

We now set

G(v,\bm{\rho})=\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}.

(7.2)

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
so that
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}({\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-S_{j}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}),d\Delta_{j}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta},2^{-j}\partial_{x}\Delta_{j}d{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.3)

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
so that
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

G(v,\bm{\rho})\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.4)

Furthermore, for each
t
it holds that

\|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt\|_{C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d{\mathbf{g}}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(7.5)

7.1.
Energy estimates on the characteristic hypersurface

Let
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
. Then the following estimates hold:

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v,\bm{\rho}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s,\infty}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\varpi|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}+\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.6)

It suffices to prove Proposition
7.1
and Proposition
7.2
for
\theta=(0,0,1)
and
r=0
. We fix this choice, and suppress
\theta
and
r
in our notation. We use
(x_{3},x^{\prime})
instead of
(x_{\theta},x^{\prime}_{\theta})
. Then
\Sigma
is defined by

\Sigma=\left\{x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})=0\right\}.

The hypothesis
G\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
implies that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\leq 2\epsilon_{1}.

(7.7)

By using Sobolev imbedding, we have

\|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\partial_{t}d\phi(t,x^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(7.8)

Let us now introduce two lemmas in Smith-Tataru’s paper
[
32
]
.

Lemma 7.3
.

[
32
]

Let
\tilde{h}(t,x)=h(t,x^{\prime},x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
. Then we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{h}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty},\quad\|d\tilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|dh\|_{{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}},\quad\|\tilde{h}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\|h\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.

Lemma 7.4
.

[
32
]

For
r\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2},\\
\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

If
r>\frac{n+1}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|hf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}.

Similarly, if
r>\frac{n}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|hf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.

Lemma 7.5
.

Suppose
U
satisfy the hyperbolic system

U_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U)U_{x_{i}}=F.

(7.9)

Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(7.10)

Proof.

Choosing the change of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\tilde{U}(t,x)=U(t,x^{\prime},x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
, the system (
7.10
) is transformed to

\partial_{t}\tilde{U}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\tilde{U}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{U}-\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{i}\tilde{U}+\tilde{F}.

For
\phi
is independent of
x_{3}
, we then update it by

\partial_{t}\tilde{U}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\tilde{U}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{U}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{i}\tilde{U}+\tilde{F}.

(7.11)

To prove (
7.10
), we first establish the
0
-order estimate. A direct calculation on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
shows that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{U}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\|d\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial d\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}.\end{split}

By using Lemma
7.3
, (
7.6
) and (
7.8
), we can prove that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}}+\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}.

(7.12)

We now establish the
s_{0}
-order estimate. Taking the derivative of
\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}
(
1\leq|\beta|\leq s_{0}
) on (
7.11
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we get

\begin{split}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma}}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{U}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\tilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\tilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\tilde{F}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+I_{1}+I_{2},\end{split}

(7.13)

where

\begin{split}&I_{1}=-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{U}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau,\\
&I_{2}=-\sum^{2}_{i=1}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{i}\tilde{U}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau.\end{split}

We can write
I_{1}
as

\begin{split}I_{1}=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{U})-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}\big)\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau\\
&+\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\phi\cdot\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau,\\
&=-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}]\tilde{U}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau\end{split}

We also write

\begin{split}I_{2}=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{i}\tilde{U})-A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{i}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau\\
&+\sum^{2}_{i=1}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(A_{i}(\tilde{U})\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\big)\cdot\partial_{i}(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U})\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}dxd\tau.\end{split}

By commutator estimates in Lemma
2.5
, we can get

\begin{split}|I_{1}|&\lesssim\big(\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}}\|d\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\|d\tilde{U}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}}\end{split}

(7.14)

and

\begin{split}|I_{2}|\lesssim&\big(\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}}\|d\partial\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\tilde{U}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}}\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}d\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}}\\
&+\big(\|d\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|\tilde{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|\partial^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.15)

Taking sum of
1\leq\beta\leq s_{0}
on (
7.13
), due to Lemma
7.3
, (
7.14
), (
7.15
), (
7.6
), and (
7.8
), we obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|dU\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|dF\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(7.16)

Taking derivatives on (
7.9
), we have

\begin{split}(\partial U)_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U)(\partial U)_{x_{i}}=-\sum^{3}_{i=1}\partial(A_{i}(U))U_{x_{i}}+\partial F,\end{split}

In a similar process, we can obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|dU\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|dF\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(7.17)

Using
\partial_{t}U=-\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U)\partial_{i}U
and Lemma
7.4
, we can carry out

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
&\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|dU\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|dF\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(7.18)

Combining (
7.12
), (
7.16
), (
7.18
), and (
7.18
), we obtain (
7.10
). Thus, the proof is finished.
∎

Using Lemma
7.5
, and combining with (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), we can obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 7.6
.

Suppose that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
. Then the following estimate

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{\rho}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}

(7.19)

holds.

The next goal is to establish the characteristic energy estimates for
\varpi
. Compared with the energy estimates for
\varpi
, the characteristic energy is along the hypersurface, not the Cauchy slices. So, it’s not trivial. To prove the energy estimates of
\varpi
along the null hypersurface, Let us first give a lemma.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
f
satisfy the following transport equation

\mathbf{T}f=F.

(7.20)

Set
L=\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
. Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

(7.21)

Proof.

Taking the operator
L
on (
7.20
), we derive that

\mathbf{T}Lf=LF+[L,\mathbf{T}]f.

Choosing the change of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\widetilde{f}=f(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
, then the above equation transforms to

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{Lf}+\tilde{v}^{i}\cdot(\partial_{x_{i}}+\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{Lf}=&\widetilde{LF}+\widetilde{[L,\mathbf{T}]f}.\end{split}

Rewrite it as

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\widetilde{Lf}+\tilde{v}^{i}\cdot\partial_{x_{i}}\widetilde{Lf}=&\widetilde{LF}+\widetilde{[L,\mathbf{T}]f}-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf}-\tilde{v}^{i}\cdot\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf}.\end{split}

(7.22)

Multiplying
\widetilde{Lf}
and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can show that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ &\big|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}LF\cdot Lfdxd\tau\big|+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(1+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ +\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\end{split}

(7.23)

Considering
L=\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
, by commutator estimates in Lemma
2.14
, we get

\begin{split}\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.24)

By elliptic estimates, we also have

\|Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}.

(7.25)

By using (
7.23
), (
7.24
), and (
7.25
), it can give us

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ \|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(1+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\big|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}LF\cdot Lfdxd\tau\big|.

(7.26)

It remains for us to estimate the high order term. Taking derivatives
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}
on (
7.22
), we have

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}+\tilde{v}^{i}\cdot\partial_{x_{i}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}=&\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\left(\widetilde{[L,\mathbf{T}]f}\right)-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf})\\
&+\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{LF}-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf})\\
&-[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}]\widetilde{Lf}.\end{split}

(7.27)

Multiplying
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}
on (
7.27
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we derive that

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}\|_{L^{2}_{\Sigma}}\lesssim&\ \|dv\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}({[L,\mathbf{T}]f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ +\big|\int^{t}_{0}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\big|+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ +\big|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})\big)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

We will estimate the right terms one by one. By using elliptic estimates, we can prove

\|dv\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}},

(7.28)

and

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}({[L,\mathbf{T}]f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\ \|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.29)

For the right term
\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}
in (
7.29
), by using Lemma
2.14
, we have

\begin{split}\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}&\lesssim\|v\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|v\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,\infty}}\cdot\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\|f\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Substituting it to (
7.29
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}({[L,\mathbf{T}]f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.30)

Using Lemma
2.16
, we also have

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq&\ \|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\partial v\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|Lf\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.31)

For
\phi
is independent with
x_{3}
, we have

\begin{split}&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
=\ &\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left([\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf})-[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf})-\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
=\ &\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left([\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf})-[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x_{3}}\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau.\end{split}

(7.32)

We will estimate the right terms on (
7.32
). For the first one and the second one, we use Lemma
2.16
and Lemma
2.13
to bound

\begin{split}&\big(\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\big(\|\partial(\partial_{t}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\partial(v\partial\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\big(\|\partial(d\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}_{x}}\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}},\end{split}

(7.33)

where we take
\beta
=
s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon_{0}>s_{0}-2
. By Sobolev imbedding, we can get

\|\partial(d\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\partial(d\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}}\lesssim\|\partial(d\phi-dt)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}(\Sigma)}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma},

and

\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}_{x}}\leq 1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}_{x}}\lesssim 1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}.

Substituting them to (
7.33
), we can update (
7.33
) as

\begin{split}&\big(\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big(|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\big).\end{split}

(7.34)

For the third term on the right hand of (
7.32
), we can bound it by

\begin{split}&\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x_{3}}\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\right|\\
\lesssim&\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|Lf\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.35)

Sustituting (
7.34
) and (
7.35
), we can estimate (
7.32
) by

\begin{split}&\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})\big)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\right|\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big(|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\big)\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big(|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}})(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\big),\end{split}

(7.36)

where we use the fact that
\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}\leq\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}
.
Combining (
7.28
) to (
7.36
), we can get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

(7.37)

Adding (
7.26
) and (
7.37
), we can derive

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

Therefore, we complete the proof of Lemma
7.7
.
∎

We also need to give the estimate of
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
along the characteristic hypersurfaces, for
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
is a nonlinear term in the wave equation of the velocity. Then,
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
decides the regularity of the characteristic hypersurfaces.

Lemma 7.8
.

Suppose that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
. Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{curl}\varpi|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(7.38)

Proof.

Consider only
\mathrm{curl}\Omega
satisfying a good transport equation. So we should transfer the goal on
\Omega
. By product estimates on
H^{s}(\Sigma^{t})
(see Lemma
2.6
) and trace theorem, we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{curl}\varpi|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}=&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
=&\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\partial_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\Omega)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}}}\|\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}}\|\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}}}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}{\bm{\rho}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}}\|\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}}}\|\partial_{t}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}}\\
\lesssim&(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\bm{\rho}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Omega|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

(7.39)

By Definition (
7.1
), we can see

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Omega|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}=\|\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial_{t}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.

(7.40)

In the following, we will give the bound of the right terms on (
7.40
). We divide it into several steps.

Step 1:
\|\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. Note (
2.6
).
We can simply write it as

\mathbf{T}\Omega=\partial v\cdot\partial\varpi.

(7.41)

By changing of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
, multiplying
\Omega
and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\Omega\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\ \|\partial v\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}})\|\Omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Using
\Omega=\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi
, (
6.4
), and (
6.5
), we derive that

\|\Omega\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon^{3}_{2}.

(7.42)

It remains for us to bound
\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. Let us first estimate
\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. By Hodge decomposition,

\begin{split}\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&=\|L(\mathrm{curl}\Omega)+H(\mathrm{div}\Omega)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\leq\|L(\mathrm{curl}\Omega)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|H(\mathrm{div}\Omega)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)},\end{split}

(7.43)

where the operators
L
and
H
are given by
\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
and
\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla
respectively. By Sobolev imbedding
H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and
s-s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}>\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}H(\mathrm{div}\Omega)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}H(\mathrm{div}\Omega)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathrm{div}\Omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\Omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.44)

We then update (
7.44
) by

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}H(\mathrm{div}\Omega)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&\ \lesssim\|\Omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(7.45)

In a similar way, we can deduce

\begin{split}\|H(\Omega\cdot\partial\bm{\rho})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&=\|H(\Omega\cdot\partial\bm{\rho})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}},\quad\\
&\ \lesssim\|\Omega\cdot\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\Omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&\ \lesssim\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(7.46)

Adding (
7.45
) and (
7.46
), we can conclude that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|H(\mathrm{div}\Omega)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{2}.

(7.47)

It remains for us to estimate
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\mathrm{curl}\Omega)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}
. Recall the transport equation for
\mathrm{curl}\Omega
:

\begin{split}&\mathbf{T}\big(\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\big)\\
=&\ \partial^{i}\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)+R^{i}_{1}+R^{i}_{2}+R^{i}_{3}+R^{i}_{4}+R^{i}_{5}+R^{i}_{6}.\end{split}

(7.48)

We set

f^{i}=\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a},\quad i=1,2,3,

and

F^{i}=\sum^{6}_{k=1}R^{i}_{k},\qquad G=2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a},\quad i=1,2,3.

Here
R_{1},R_{2},\ldots,R_{6}
is defined in (
2.9
). Then we can write (
7.48
) as

\mathbf{T}f^{i}=F^{i}+\partial^{i}G.

(7.49)

Operating the operator
L
on (
7.49
), and using Lemma
7.7
, we can deduce that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ &\ \big(\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{LF^{i}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{Lf_{i}}dxd\tau\right|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\widetilde{LG}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{Lf_{i}}dxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

(7.50)

We set

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}&=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{LF^{i}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{Lf_{i}}dxd\tau\right|,\\
\mathrm{J}&=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\widetilde{LG}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{Lf_{i}}dxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

Then we have
For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, we use Hölder’s inequality to give the bound

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}\leq&\ \textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}LF\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}L(\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \sum^{3}_{i=1}\sum^{6}_{j=1}\|R^{i}_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.51)

Recall the expressions for
R^{i}_{1},R^{i}_{2},...R^{i}_{6}
in (
2.9
), we get

\begin{split}&\sum^{3}_{i=1}\sum^{6}_{j=1}\|R^{i}_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\lesssim(\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|dv,\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\|_{H^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(7.52)

We also get

\|\mathrm{curl}\Omega-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{2}}\|\varpi\|_{H^{2}}.

(7.53)

Inserting (
7.52
) and (
7.53
) into (
7.54
) and using (
6.3
)-(
6.5
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}\lesssim&\ \epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(7.54)

As for
\mathrm{J}
, we separate it as

\mathrm{J}\leq\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}(|J^{1}|+|J^{2}|+|J^{3}|+|J^{4}|),

where

\begin{split}J_{1}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{LG})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}dxd\tau,\\
J_{2}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3}](\widetilde{LG})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}dxd\tau,\\
J_{3}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}dxd\tau,\\
J_{4}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3}]\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}dxd\tau.\end{split}

Let us bound the above terms one by one. For
J_{1}
, we have

\begin{split}J_{1}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{LG})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}dxd\tau\\
=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{LG})\cdot(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\big\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}\big\}dxd\tau\\
=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{LG})\cdot[\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}]\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{LG})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\big\{(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}\big\}dxd\tau.\end{split}

Using the fact

(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}=(\partial^{i}L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))=0,

then

\begin{split}J_{1}=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{LG})\cdot[\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}]\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\Omega_{i})}dxd\tau.\end{split}

For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, by Hölder’s inequality and commutator estimates in Lemma
2.16
, we can derive
