\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}(|J_{1}|+|J_{2}|)=&\|LG\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\sigma}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|L\mathrm{curl}\Omega\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\sigma}}\\
\lesssim&\|v\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\varpi\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\varpi\|_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(7.55)

For
J_{3}
, we use Plancherel formula in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that

\begin{split}J_{3}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{3}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot\partial^{i}\phi\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{3}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\bm{\rho}}\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}\varpi_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\partial^{i}\phi\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial_{i}\varpi^{a}\big)}\right)dxd\tau.\end{split}

(7.56)

For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, by Hölder inequality, we can obtain

\begin{split}&\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{3}|\\
=&\|\partial v\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}+\|d\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\|\partial v\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\|\partial\bm{\rho}\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|v\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|\varpi\|_{H^{s_{0}}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\end{split}

By using (
6.3
)-(
6.5
), we get

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{3}|\lesssim&\ \epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(7.57)

Substituing (
7.55
), …(
7.57
) to (
7.50
) and using (
6.3
)-(
6.5
), we can update (
7.50
) by

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(7.58)

For

Lf^{i}=L(\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i})-2L(\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}).

we then get

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\geq|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\mathrm{curl}\Omega^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}-2|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\left(\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\right)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}.

(7.59)

By trace theorem and elliptic estimate, we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\left(\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\right)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\leq&\ C\|L\left(\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\right)\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}+(s-s_{0})}_{x}}\\
\leq&\ C\|\mathrm{e}^{-{\bm{\rho}}}\partial_{a}\bm{\rho}\partial^{i}\varpi^{a}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}+(s-s_{0})}_{x}}\\
\leq&\ C\|\partial\bm{\rho}\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(7.60)

Sustituing (
7.59
) and (
7.60
) to (
7.58
), we therefore get

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\mathrm{curl}\Omega)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ \epsilon^{2}_{2}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{2}.

(7.61)

If adding (
7.61
) to (
7.47
), then we have

\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{2}.

(7.62)

Due to

\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Omega=\partial\Omega\cdot(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}},

by Hölder inequality and product estimates in Lemma
2.6
, we can deduce the following estimate

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&\lesssim\|\partial\Omega\cdot(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(7.63)

Above, we use the trace theorem

\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}
.
Through (
7.42
) and (
7.63
), we can see

\|\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.64)

Step 2:
\|\partial_{t}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. By using (
2.6
) and Sobolev imbeddings, we also have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&\leq\|-(v\cdot\nabla)\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial v\cdot\partial W\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\leq\|(v\cdot\nabla)\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial v\cdot\partial\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\leq\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial v\cdot\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}},\\
&\leq\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}(\Sigma)}\|\partial\Omega\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\partial\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.65)

Due to (
7.40
), (
7.64
), (
7.65
), and (
6.3
)-(
6.5
), we can show that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Omega|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.66)

We finally use (
7.39
) and (
7.66
) to conclude that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{curl}\varpi|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

∎

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
U=(\bm{\rho},v)^{\mathrm{T}}
satisfy the assumption in Lemma
7.5
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(U-S_{j}U),dS_{j}U,2^{-j}d\partial_{x}S_{j}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.67)

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
be a standard multiplier of order
0
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, such that
\Delta_{0}
is additionally bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Clearly,

(\Delta_{0}U)_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A_{i}(U)(\Delta_{0}U)_{x_{i}}=-\sum^{3}_{i=1}[\Delta_{0},A_{i}(U)]\partial_{x_{i}}U.

Set
G=-\sum^{3}_{i=1}[\Delta_{0},A_{i}(U)]\partial_{x_{i}}U
.
Due to Lemma
7.5
, we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}U\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|G\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\|\Delta_{0}U\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}.

(7.68)

Using commutator estimates, we can prove

\|G\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}U\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}.

In a result, we can update (
7.68
) by

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}U\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}.

(7.69)

To control the norm of
2^{j}(U-S_{j}U)
, we write

2^{j}(U-S_{j}U)=2^{j}\sum_{m\geq j}\Delta_{m}U,

where
\Delta_{m}U
satisfies the above conditions for
\Delta_{0}U
. Applying (
7.69
) and replacing
s_{0}
to
s_{0}-1
, we get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(U-S_{j}U)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\sum_{m\geq j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{m}\Delta_{m}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\sum_{m\geq j}(2^{m}\|\Delta_{m}U\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}})\\
\lesssim&\|dU\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|U\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

Taking square of it, we can see

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(U-S_{j}U)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Finally, applying (
7.68
) to
\Delta_{0}=S_{j}
and
\Delta_{0}=2^{-j}\partial_{x}S_{j}
shows that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|dS_{j}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{-j}d\partial_{x}S_{j}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
7.9
is completed.
∎

We are now ready to give a precise proof of Proposition
7.1
.

proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Note
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
. Using Lemma
7.9
,
it suffices for us to verify that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

By Lemma
7.5
and (
7.6
)-(
7.8
), we have

\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\bm{\rho}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Noting the expression of
\mathbf{g}
, by Lemma
7.4
, we derive that

\begin{split}\|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|v\cdot v|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|c_{s}^{2}(\bm{\rho})-c_{s}^{2}(0)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Thus, the conclusion of Proposition
7.1
holds.
∎

7.2.
The null frame

We introduce a null frame along
\Sigma
as follows. Let

V=(dr)^{*},

where
r
is the defining function of the foliation
\Sigma
, and where
*
denotes the identification of covectors and vectors induced by
\mathbf{g}
. Then
V
is the null geodesic flow field tangent to
\Sigma
. Let

\sigma=dt(V),\qquad l=\sigma^{-1}V.

(7.70)

Thus
l
is the g-normal field to
\Sigma
normalized so that
dt(l)=1
, hence

l=\left<dt,dx_{3}-d\phi\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}\left(dx_{3}-d\phi\right)^{*},

(7.71)

so the coefficients
l^{j}
are smooth functions of
v,\rho
and
d\phi
. Conversely,

dx_{3}-d\phi=\left<l,\partial_{x_{3}}\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}l^{*},

(7.72)

so that
d\phi
is a smooth function of
v,\rho
and the coefficients of
l
.

Next we introduce the vector fields
e_{a},a=1,2
tangent to the fixed-time slice
\Sigma^{t}
of
\Sigma
. We do this by applying Grahm-Schmidt orthogonalization in the metric
\mathbf{g}
to the
\Sigma^{t}
-tangent vector fields
\partial_{x_{a}}+\partial_{x_{a}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}},a=1,2
.

Finally, we let

\underline{l}=l+2\partial_{t}.

It follows that
\{l,\underline{l},e_{1},e_{2}\}
form a null frame in the sense that

\displaystyle\left<l,\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=2,\qquad\qquad\ \left<e_{a},e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\delta_{ab}\ (a,b=1,2),

\displaystyle\left<l,l\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\left<\underline{l},\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=0,\quad\left<l,e_{a}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\left<\underline{l},e_{a}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=0\ (a=1,2).

The coefficients of each of the fields is a smooth function of
u
and
d\phi
, and by assumption we also have the pointwise bound

|e_{1}-\partial_{x_{1}}|+|e_{2}-\partial_{x_{2}}|+|l-(\partial_{t}+\partial_{x_{3}})|+|\underline{l}-(-\partial_{t}+\partial_{x_{3}})|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

After that, we can state the following corollary concerning to the decomposition of curvature tensor.

Lemma 7.10
.

[
32
]

Suppose
f
satisfying

\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f=F.

Let
(t,x^{\prime},\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
denote the projective parametrisation of
\Sigma
, and for
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2
, let
/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
denote differentiation along
\Sigma
in the induced coordinates. Then, for
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2
, one can write

/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\beta}(f|_{\Sigma})=l(f_{2})+f_{1},

where

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}}+\|df\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}}+\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.

Corollary 7.11
.

Let
R
be the Riemann curvature tensor for the metric
{\mathbf{g}}
. Let
e_{0}=l
. Then for any
0\leq a,b,c,d\leq 2
, we can write

\left<R(e_{a},e_{b})e_{c},e_{d}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}|_{\Sigma}=l(f_{2})+f_{1},

(7.73)

where
|f_{1}|\lesssim|\mathrm{curl}\varpi|+|d\mathbf{g}|^{2}
,
|f_{2}|\lesssim|d{\mathbf{g}}|
. Moreover, the characteristic estimates

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(7.74)

holds. Additionally, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|f_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(7.75)

Proof.

According to the expression of curvature tensor, we have

\left<R(e_{a},e_{b})e_{c},e_{d}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=R_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}e^{\alpha}_{a}e^{\beta}_{b}e_{c}^{\mu}e_{d}^{\nu},

where

R_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}+\partial^{2}_{\beta\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\mu}-\partial^{2}_{\beta\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\nu}-\partial^{2}_{\alpha\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\mu}\right]+Q(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},d\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\beta}),

where
Q
is a sum of products of coefficients of
\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}
with quadratic forms in
d\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\beta}
. By using Proposition
7.1
, then
Q
satisfies the bound required of
f_{1}
. It suffices for us to consider

\frac{1}{2}e^{\alpha}_{a}e^{\beta}_{b}e_{c}^{\mu}e_{d}^{\nu}\left[\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}+\partial^{2}_{\beta\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\mu}-\partial^{2}_{\beta\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\nu}-\partial^{2}_{\alpha\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\mu}\right].

We therefore look at the term
e^{\alpha}_{a}e_{c}^{\mu}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}
, which is typical. By (
7.7
), Proposition
7.1
, and Lemma
7.7
, we get

\|l^{\alpha}-\delta^{\alpha 0}\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+\|\underline{l}^{\alpha}+\delta^{\alpha 0}-2\delta^{\alpha n}\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+\|e^{\alpha}_{a}-\delta^{\alpha a}\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(7.76)

By (
7.76
) and Proposition
7.1
, the term
e_{a}(e_{c}^{\mu})\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}
satisfies the bound required of
f_{1}
, so we consider
e_{a}(e_{c}(\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}))
. Finally, since the coefficients of
e_{c}
in the basis
/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
have tangential derivatives bounded in
L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
, we are reduced by Lemma
7.10
to verifying that

\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Note

\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}=\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}.

By Lemma
7.8
and Corollary
7.6
, we have

\begin{split}\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\simeq&\ \|\square_{\mathbf{g}}v\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|(d{\mathbf{g}})^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Above,
\mathrm{curl}\varpi
and
(d{\mathbf{g}})^{2}
is included in
f_{1}
. We hence complete the proof of Corollary
7.11
.
∎

7.3.
The estimate of connection coefficients

Define

\chi_{ab}=\left<D_{e_{a}}l,e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}},\qquad l(\ln\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}\left<D_{l}\underline{l},l\right>_{\mathbf{g}},\quad\mu_{0ab}=\left<D_{l}e_{a},e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}.

For
\sigma
, we set the initial data
\sigma=1
at the time
-2
. Thanks to Proposition
7.1
, we have

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|l(\ln\sigma)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(7.77)

In a similar way, if we expand
l=l^{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
in the tangent frame
\partial_{t},\partial_{x^{\prime}}
on
\Sigma
, then

l^{0}=1,\quad\|l^{1}\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(7.78)

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
\chi
be defined as before. Then

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(7.79)

Furthermore, for any
t\in[-2,2]
,

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(7.80)

Proof.

The well-known transport equation for
\chi
along null hypersurfaces (see references
[
22
]
and
[
32
]
) can be described as

l(\chi_{ab})=\left<R(l,e_{a})l,e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}-\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}+\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}+\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac}.

Due to Corollary
7.11
, we can write the above equation in the following

l(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})=f_{1}-\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}+\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}+\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac},

(7.81)

where

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(7.82)

and for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|f_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(7.83)

Let
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}
be the fractional derivative operator in the
x^{\prime}
variables. Set

G=f_{1}-\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}+\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}+\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac}.

We have

\begin{split}&\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
\lesssim\ &\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-1},l](\chi_{ab}-f_{2})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}G\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.\end{split}

(7.84)

A direct calculation shows that

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s-1}G\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\chi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&\quad+\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\cdot\|l(\ln\sigma)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&\quad+\|\mu\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\cdot\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})},\end{split}

(7.85)

where we use the fact that
H^{s-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
is an algebra for
s>2
.

We next bound

\displaystyle\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-1},l](\chi_{ab}-f_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\|/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}l^{\alpha}(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}

\displaystyle\quad\ +\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha},l^{\alpha}](\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.

By Kato-Ponce commutator estimate and Sobolev embeddings, the above is bounded by

\|l^{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.

(7.86)

Gathering (
7.77
), (
7.78
), (
7.82
) and (
7.84
)-(
7.86
) together, we thus prove that

\sup_{t}\|(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

From (
7.81
), we see that

\begin{split}\|\chi_{ab}-f_{2}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}&\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad+\|\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(7.87)

By Sobolev imbedding
H^{s_{0}-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C^{\delta}(\mathbb{R}),\delta\in(0,s_{0}-2)
and Gronwall’s inequality, we derive that

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

∎

7.4.
The proof of Proposition
7.2

Note

G(v,\bm{\rho})=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s,2,\Sigma}.

Using (
7.72
) and the estimate of
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}
in Proposition
7.1
, then the estimate (
7.4
) follows from the bound

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

where it is understood that one takes the norm of the coefficients of
l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})
in the standard frame on
\mathbb{R}^{3+1}
. The geodesic equation, together with the bound for Christoffel symbols
\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}
, imply that

\|l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

so it suffices to bound the tangential derivatives of the coefficients of
l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})
in the norm
L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
. By using Proposition
7.1
, we can estimate the Christoffel symbols

\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Note that
H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
is a algebra if
s_{0}>2
. We then deduce that

\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}e_{1}^{\beta}l^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

We are now in a position to establish the following bound,

\|\left<D_{e_{a}}l,e_{b}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{e_{a}}l,\underline{l}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{l}l,\underline{l}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

The first term is
\chi_{ab}
which is bounded by Lemma
7.12
. For the second term, noting

\left<D_{e_{a}}l,\underline{l}\right>=\left<D_{e_{a}}l,2\partial_{t}\right>=-2\left<D_{e_{a}}\partial_{t},l\right>,

then it can be bounded via Proposition
7.1
. Similarly, we can control the last term through Proposition
7.1
. At this stage, it remains for us to show that

\|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt\|_{C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

To do that, it suffices for us to show that

\|l(t,\cdot)-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})\|_{C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

The coefficients of
e_{1}
are small in
C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
perturbations of their constant coefficient analogs, so it suffices to show that

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,e_{1}\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,\underline{l}\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Above, the first term is bounded by Lemma
7.12
, and the second by using

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}\partial_{t},l\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Consequently, we complete the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

8.
Strichartz estimates for solutions

In this part, let us introce the Strichartz estimates of linear wave equations.

Proposition 8.1
.

Suppose that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
.
For any
1\leq r\leq s_{0}+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[0,T]
, the linear, non-homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}f=\mathbf{T}G+B,\qquad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{0}\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad\partial_{t}f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{1}\in H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\end{cases}

admites a solution
f\in C([0,T],H^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T],H^{r-1})
and the following estimates holds:

\|f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{r-1}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}.

(8.1)

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
k<r-1
,

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}\cap L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}.

(8.2)

Proposition 8.2
.

Suppose that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. For each
1\leq r\leq s_{0}+1
, then the linear, homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}F=0,\\
&F(t_{0},\cdot)=F_{0},\quad\partial_{t}F(t_{0},\cdot)=F_{1},\end{cases}

is well-posed for the initial data
(F_{0},F_{1})
in
H^{r}\times H^{r-1}
. Moreover, the solution satisfies, respectively, the energy estimates

\|F\|_{H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}F\|_{H^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|F_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|F_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}\big),

and the Strichartz estimates

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|F_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|F_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}\big).

(8.3)

We will use Proposition
8.2
to prove Proposition
8.1
by Lemma
2.12
.

proof of Proposition
8.1
by Proposition
8.2
.

Let
\mathbf{g}
be defined in Proposition
8.1
. Let
V
satisfy the the linear, homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}V=0,\\
&V(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{0},\quad\mathbf{T}V(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{1}-G(t_{0}),\end{cases}

(8.4)

where
(f_{0},f_{1}-G(t_{0}))\in H^{r}\times H^{r-1}
.
Let
Q
satisfy the the linear, nonhomogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}Q=\mathbf{T}G+B,\\
&Q(t_{0},\cdot)=0,\quad\mathbf{T}Q(t_{0},\cdot)=G(t_{0}),\end{cases}

(8.5)

where
G(0)\in H^{r-1}
. To write
V
and
Q
adapting to Proposition
8.2
, we can rewrite (
8.4
) and (
8.5
) by using
\mathbf{T}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
, respectively,

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}V=0,\\
&V(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}V(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{1}-G(t_{0})-v\cdot\nabla f_{0}(t_{0},\cdot),\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}Q=\mathbf{T}G+B,\\
&Q(t_{0},\cdot)=0,\quad\partial_{t}Q(t_{0},\cdot)=G(t_{0}),\end{cases}

By superposition principle for linear wave equation, then

f=V+Q
satisfying

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=\mathbf{T}G+B,\qquad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{0}\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad\mathbf{T}f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{1}\in H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).\end{cases}

To prove (
8.1
) and (
8.2
), we transfer the goal to bound
V
and
Q
. By Proposition
8.2
, we can see

\|V\|_{H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}V\|_{H^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}-G(t_{0})-v\cdot\nabla V(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H^{r-1}}\big),

and

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}V\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}-G(t_{0})-v\cdot\nabla V(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H^{r-1}}\big),\quad k<r-1.

Using
s_{0}>2
and
r<s_{0}+1
, then we can obtain
we can see

\begin{split}\|V\|_{H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}V\|_{H^{r-1}}\leq&C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G(t_{0})\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|v\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|\nabla f_{0}(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H^{r-1}}\big)\\
\leq&C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big),\end{split}

(8.6)

and

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}V\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big),\quad k<r-1.

(8.7)

By Lemma
2.12
and using Proposition
8.2
again, we can prove

\|Q\|_{H^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}Q\|_{H^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|G(t_{0})\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big),

(8.8)

and

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}V\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|G(t_{0})\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big),\quad k<r-1.

(8.9)

Adding (
8.6
) and (
8.8
), we get (
8.1
). Adding (
8.7
) and (
8.9
), we get (
8.2
). Therefore, we finish the proof of Proposition
8.1
.
∎

Based on Proposition
8.1
, we can derive the following result.

Proposition 8.3
.

Suppose
(v,\rho,\varpi)\in\mathcal{H}
and
G(v,\rho)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Let
v_{+}
be defined in (
1.13
). We have

\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|\partial v_{+},d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(8.10)

and

\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\|\varpi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(8.11)

Proof.

Recall
\bm{\rho}
and
v_{+}
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bm{\rho}=\mathcal{D},\\
&\square_{\mathbf{g}}v_{+}=\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\eta+\mathcal{R}.\end{split}

(8.12)

Using Lemma
2.18
, we get

\|\mathcal{D},\mathcal{R}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-1}}\lesssim\|dv,d\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|dv,d{\bm{\rho}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}}.

Operating
\Delta_{j}
on (
8.12
), we can derive that
\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}
and
\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}
satisfy

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}=\Delta_{j}\mathcal{D}+[\square_{g},\Delta_{j}]\bm{\rho},\\
&\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}_{0},\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}\eta^{i}+\Delta_{j}Q^{i}+[\square_{g},\Delta_{j}]v^{i}_{+}+[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}]\mathbf{T}\eta^{i},\\
&\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}(v_{0}-\eta_{0}),\quad\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}v^{i}_{+}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}(v_{0}-\eta_{0}).\end{cases}

By using the Strichartz estimate in Proposition
8.2
(taking
r=s-s_{0}+1,k=0
) , we obtain

\begin{split}\|(\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho},\Delta_{j}v_{+})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\ &\|\Delta_{j}\bm{\rho}(0,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-s_{0}+1}}+\|\Delta_{j}(v_{0}-\eta_{0})\|_{H^{s-s_{0}+1}}+\|\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}\eta(0,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-s_{0}+1}}\\
&+\|\Delta_{j}Q\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-s_{0}}}+\|[\square_{g},\Delta_{j}]\bm{\rho}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-s_{0}}}+\|[\square_{g},\Delta_{j}]v_{+}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-s_{0}}}\\
&+\|[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}]\mathbf{T}\eta\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-s_{0}}}+\|\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}\eta\|_{H_{x}^{s-s_{0}+1}}+\|\Delta_{j}\mathcal{D}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s-s_{0}}}\end{split}

(8.13)

Multiplying
2^{(s_{0}-1)j}
on (
8.13
), and taking square of it and summing it over
j\geq 1
, we get

\begin{split}\|\bm{\rho},v_{+}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}&\lesssim\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|v_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{T}\eta_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+{\|\varpi_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}}.\end{split}

(8.14)

By Lemma
2.18
, we also update (
8.14
) by

\|\bm{\rho},v_{+}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|v_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+{\|\varpi_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}}.

In a result, we have

\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+{\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

To get the Strichartz estimates for
v
, let us recall it’s relation with
v_{+}
and
\bm{\rho}
. That is,

v=\eta+v_{+},\quad\eta=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi).

We then get

\begin{split}\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\|\partial v_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\eta\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}.\end{split}

(8.15)

To bound
\|\partial v\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}
, we need first bound
\|\partial\eta\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}
. Note

\partial\eta=\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi).

By Sobolev inequality and elliptic estimate, we have

\begin{split}\|\partial\eta\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}&\lesssim\|\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi)\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\varpi\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\\
&\lesssim(1+\|\bm{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}})\|\varpi_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(8.16)

Combining (
8.15
) and (
8.16
), we can see

\|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\partial v_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\eta\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

We hence prove that

\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v,\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(8.17)

Using the original transport equation (
1.11
):

\mathbf{T}\bm{\rho}=-\mathrm{div}v,\quad\mathbf{T}v=-c^{2}_{s}\partial\bm{\rho},

we then obtain

\|\mathbf{T}\bm{\rho},\mathbf{T}v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\partial\bm{\rho},\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(8.18)

Combining (
8.17
) and (
8.18
) together, we have

\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(8.19)

It also remains for us to bound
\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}
and
\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}
. By Littlewood-Palay decomposition, we also have

\begin{split}\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\\
\lesssim&\left\{2^{j(s_{0}-2)}(\Delta_{j}d\bm{\rho},\Delta_{j}dv)\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\left\{2^{-j(s_{0}-2)}\right\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Similarly, for
\delta\in(0,s_{0}-2)
, we get

\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\ \|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{\delta}_{\infty,2}}+\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}.

Therefore, we have

\|dv,d\bm{\rho},\partial v_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\bm{\rho},dv\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Using Theorem
3.3
, we can obtain (
8.11
). At this stage, we have finished the proof of Proposition
8.3
. The only task is Proposition
8.2
, which will be stated in the next section.
∎

9.
Appendix: proof of Proposition
8.2

Following Smith-Tataru’s paper
[
32
]
, we will give a proof of Proposition
8.2
in this part. We divide the proofs into several steps. The first step is to reduce the problem in the phase space.

9.1.
A reduction to the paradifferential decomposition

Given a frequency scale
\lambda\geq 1
, we consider the smooth coefficients

\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}=S_{<\lambda}\mathbf{g}.

We can reduce the Proposition
8.2
to the following:

Proposition 9.1
.

Suppose that
(v,\bm{\rho},\varpi)\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(v,\bm{\rho})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Suppose
f
satisfy

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,\\
f|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}.\end{cases}

(9.1)

Then for each
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
there exists a function
f_{\lambda}\in C^{\infty}([-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with

\mathrm{supp}\widehat{f_{\lambda}(t,\cdot)}\subseteq\{\xi:\frac{\lambda}{8}\leq|\xi|\leq 8\lambda\},

such that

\begin{cases}&\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}),\\
&f_{\lambda}(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f_{\lambda}(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{1}.\end{cases}

(9.2)

Additionally, if
r>1
, then the following Strichartz estimates holds:

\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{r-1}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

(9.3)

proof of Proposition
8.2
by Proposition
9.1
.

We divide the proof into several cases.

(i)
r=1
. Using basic energy estimates for (
1.19
), we have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\ (\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}})\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
\lesssim&\ \|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Then the Cauchy problem (
1.19
) holds a unique solution
f\in C([0,T],H^{1})
and
\partial_{t}f\in C([0,T],L^{2})
. It remains to show that the solution
f
also satisfies the Strichartz estimate (
8.3
).

Without loss of generality, we take
t_{0}=0
. For any given initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
, and
t_{0}\in[-2,2]
, we take a Littlewood-Paley decomposition

f_{0}=\sum_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}f_{0},\qquad f_{1}=\sum_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}f_{1},

and for each
\lambda
we take the corresponding
f_{\lambda}
as in (
9.2
). If we set

f=\sum_{\lambda}f_{\lambda},

then
f
matches the initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})
at the time
t=t_{0}
, and also satisfies the Strichartz estimates (
9.3
). In fact,
f
is also an approximate solution for
\square_{\mathbf{g}}
in the sense that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

We can derive the above bound by using the decomposition

\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}+\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}.

The first one can be controlled by Proposition
9.1
. As for the second one, noting
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, we then have

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}=\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}({\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}})\partial df_{\lambda}.\end{split}

It follows from Hölder inequality that

\begin{split}\|\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\left(\lambda\|{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\left(\sum_{\lambda}\|df_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(9.4)

On the other hand, we known

\begin{split}\mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\left(\lambda\|{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\lesssim&\ \mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\big(\lambda\sum_{\mu>\lambda}\|\mathbf{g}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\\
\lesssim&\ \mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\big(\lambda\sum_{\mu>\lambda}\mu^{-(1+\delta)}\|d\mathbf{g}_{\mu}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}\big)\\
\lesssim&\ \|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}(\textstyle{\sum_{\mu}}\mu^{-\delta})\lesssim\ \|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}.\end{split}

(9.5)

Gathering (
9.4
) and (
9.5
), we can prove that

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).\end{split}

For given
F\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
, we now form the function

\mathbf{M}F=\int^{t}_{0}f^{\tau}(t,x)d\tau,

where
f^{\tau}(t,x)
is the approximate solution formed above with the Cauchy data

f^{\tau}(\tau,x)=0,\quad\partial_{t}f^{\tau}(\tau,x)=F(\tau,\cdot).

By calculating

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}F=\int^{t}_{0}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{\tau}(t,x)d\tau+F,

it follows that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}F-F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{\tau}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

Using the contraction principle, we can write the solution
f
in the form

f=\tilde{f}+\mathbf{M}F,

where
\tilde{f}
is the approximation solution formed above for initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})
specified at time
t=0
, and

\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

The Strichartz estimates now follow since they holds for each
f^{\tau}
,
\tau\in[0,t]
. By Duhamel’s principle, we can also obtain the Strichartz estimates for the linear, nonhomogeneous wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=G,\\
f|_{t=0}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f|_{t=0}=f_{1}.\end{cases}

That is,

\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}},\quad\theta<0.

(ii)
1<r\leq s_{0}+1
. Based on the above result, we transform the initial data in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
. Operating
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}
on (
9.1
), we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f.

Let
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=h
. Then
h
is a solution to

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}h=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}h,\\
(h(t_{0}),\partial_{t}h(t_{0}))\in H^{1}\times L^{2}.\end{cases}

(9.6)

To handle this case, we then need to estimate the right term as

\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}h\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{m}dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}),\quad\mathrm{for}\ m>1-s_{0}.

To see this. we apply analytic interpolation to the family

h\rightarrow[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}h.

For
\mathrm{Re}z=0
, noting
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, we use the commutator estimate (c.f. (3.6.35) of
[
20
]
) to get

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>^{z}]\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

For
\mathrm{Re}z=s_{0}
, we use the Kato-Ponce commutator estimate

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>^{z}]\left<\partial\right>^{-z}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\eta^{\alpha\beta}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{-z}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},

(9.7)

where
\alpha\neq 0
because
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
. Considering

\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\eta^{\alpha\beta}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

then (
9.7
) becomes

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>^{z}]\left<\partial\right>^{-z}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{-z}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}).

Let us go back (
9.6
). Using the discussion in case
r=1
, we then get the Strichartz estimates for
h
:

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}h\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{-r}\partial dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}),\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad\theta<0.\end{split}

(9.8)

Substituting
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=h
to (
9.8
), and using the energy estimate

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}},

we can see derive that

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}),\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad k<r-1.\end{split}

∎

9.2.
The construction of wave packets

Definition 9.1

(
[
32
]
)
.

Let the hypersurface
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
and the geodesic
\gamma
be defined in Section 5. A normalized wave packet around
\gamma
is a function
f
of the form

f=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1}T_{\lambda}(uh),

where

u(t,x)=\delta(x_{\theta}-\phi_{\theta,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})),\quad h=h_{0}((\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}(x^{\prime}_{\theta}-\gamma^{\prime}_{\theta}(t))).

Here,
h_{0}
is a smooth function supported in the set
|x^{\prime}|\leq 1
, with uniform bounds on its derivatives
|\partial^{\alpha}_{x^{\prime}}h_{0}(x^{\prime})|\leq c_{\alpha}
.

We give two notations here. We denote
L(u,h)
to denote a translation invariant bilinear operator of the form

L(u,h)(x)=\int K(y,z)u(x+y)h(x+z)dydz,

where
K(y,z)
is a finite measure. If
X
is a Sobolev spaces, we then denote
X_{a}
the same space but with the norm obtained by dimensionless rescaling by
a
,

\|u\|_{X_{a}}=\|u(a\cdot)\|_{X}.

Since
2(s_{0}-1)>2
, then for
a<1
we have
\|u\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
.

9.2.1.
A normalized wave packet

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
u
be a normalized packet. Then there is another normalized wave packet
\tilde{u}
, and functions
\phi_{m}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta}),m=0,1,2
, so that

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f=L(d\mathbf{g},d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f})+\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1}P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\sum_{m=0,1,2}\psi_{m}\delta^{(m)}(x^{\prime}_{\theta}-\phi_{\theta,r}),

(9.9)

where the functions
\psi_{m}=\psi_{m}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
satisfy the scaled Sobolev estimates

\|\psi_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{a,x^{\prime}_{\theta}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\lambda^{1-m},\quad m=0,1,2,\quad a=(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(9.10)

As a immediate consequence, we can obtain

Corollary 9.3
.

Let
f
be a normalized wave packet. Then

\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim 1,\quad\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}P_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(9.11)

proof of Proposition
9.2
.

For brevity, we consider the case
\theta=(0,0,1)
. Then
x_{\theta}=x_{3}
, and
x^{\prime}_{\theta}=x^{\prime}
. We write

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f=\lambda^{-1}([\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]+P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}})(uh).

(9.12)

For the first term in (
9.12
), noting
\mathbf{g}\lambda
supported at frequency
\leq\frac{\lambda}{8}
, then we can write

[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]=[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{T}_{\lambda}

for some multipliers
\tilde{P}_{\lambda},\tilde{T}_{\lambda}
which have the same properties as
P_{\lambda},T_{\lambda}
. Therefore, by using the kernal bounds for
P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}
, we conclude that

[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]f=L(d\mathbf{g},df).

For the second term in (
9.12
), we use the Leibniz rule

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(uh)=h\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}u+(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\beta}h+u\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}h.

(9.13)

We let
\nu
denote the conormal vector field along
\Sigma
,
\nu=dx_{3}-d\phi(t,x^{\prime})
. In the following, we take the greek indices
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2
.

For the first term in (
9.13
), by calculation, we have

\begin{split}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}u=&\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(2)}_{x_{3}-\phi}-2(\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(1)}_{x_{3}-\phi}\\
&+(\partial^{2}_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(0)}_{x_{3}-\phi}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\delta^{(1)}_{x_{3}-\phi}\\
&+\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\delta^{(0)}_{x_{3}-\phi}.\end{split}

Here,
\delta^{(m)}_{x_{3}-\phi}=(\partial^{m}\delta)(x_{3}-\phi)
. By Leibniz rule, we can take

\begin{split}\psi_{0}&=h\big\{(\partial^{2}_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}+(\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\big\},\\
\psi_{1}&=h\big\{2(\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\big\},\\
\psi_{2}&=h(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta},\end{split}

By using (
7.4
), Proposition
7.1
, and Corollary
7.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},
and
\psi_{2}
satisfy the estimates (
9.10
). For the second term in (
9.12
), we have

\begin{split}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\beta}h=&\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h\delta^{(1)}_{x_{3}-\phi}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}\partial_{3}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h\delta^{(0)}_{x_{3}-\phi}.\end{split}

We then take

\psi_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}\partial_{3}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h,\quad\psi_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h.

By using (
7.4
), Proposition
7.1
, and Corollary
7.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
satisfy the estimates (
9.10
).
For the third term in (
9.12
), we can take

\psi_{0}=\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}h.

By using (
7.4
), Proposition
7.1
, and Corollary
7.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0}
satisfies the estimates (
9.10
).
∎

9.2.2.
Superpositions of wave packets

Proposition 9.4

(
[
32
]
)
.

Let
f=\sum_{\theta,j}a_{\theta,j}f^{\theta,j}
, where
f^{\theta,j}
are normalized wave packets supported in
T_{\theta,j}
. Then

\begin{split}\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}&\lesssim(\sum_{\theta,j}a^{2}_{\theta,j})^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\theta,j}a^{2}_{\theta,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(9.14)

9.2.3.
Matching the initial data

Proposition 9.5

(
[
32
]
)
.

Given any initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
, there exists a function of the form

f=\sum_{\theta,j}a_{\theta,j}f^{\theta,j},

where the function
f^{\theta,j}
are normalized wave packets, such that

P_{\lambda}f(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}P_{\lambda}f(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{1}.

Furthermore,

\sum_{\theta,j}a_{\theta,j}^{2}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

9.3.
Strichartz estimate

The estimate (
9.3
) can be directly derived by

Proposition 9.6

(
[
32
]
)
.

Let

f=\sum_{K\in\mathcal{T}}a_{K}\chi_{K},

where
\sum_{K\in\mathcal{T}}a_{K}^{2}\leq 1
. Then

\|f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\lambda)^{3}.
